## Summary

Snapback 4 is one of two parallel candidates for the successor to Snapback 2,
and the sibling of Snapback 3, not its successor. It builds
[LLP 2000.000](./2000.000-keep-the-core-flip-two-substrates.rfc.md) as
written: the Snapback 2 core kept verbatim, two substrates flipped up front —
resources metered at runtime instead of priced statically, and a client that
replicates the viewer's declared partition instead of caching receipted
results — proved first on the hub fixture, with a deterministic simulator as
the primary harness. Snapback 3 ([LLP 2000](./2000-snapback3.rfc.md), led by
Codex / GPT-6 Astra) keeps the same two questions open and discovers their
answers in its first slice. Snapback 2 ([LLP 1000](./1000-snapback2.explainer.md))
continues as the incumbent and the control. All three are judged by one
pre-registered comparison on one clock.

This document records the author's decisions of 2026-09-12 that created the
track, restates the bet on one page so a reader can orient without the brief,
fixes the bakeoff protocol, draws the fence, and plans the first four weeks.
The design argument and its evidence stay in LLP 2000.000. Only running code
will bind (`rules/RULES.md`).

## 1. Decisions (author, 2026-09-12)

On 2026-09-12 Charlie Cheever asked whether Claude Fable 5.1 should lead a
Snapback 4 while Codex / GPT-6 Astra leads Snapback 3 and Snapback 2
continues, "and see which one turns out best." The lead answered yes with
conditions; the author accepted them ("Sounds good") and asked for this
document. The decisions below are therefore the lead's proposal as accepted
by the author. Where the author has not separately ruled, the text says so.

1. **Three tracks, one comparison.** Snapback 2 continues as the incumbent
   and the control. Snapback 3 builds LLP 2000; Snapback 4 builds LLP
   2000.000. This is a bakeoff of *bets* — discover the substrates in the
   slice versus decide them first — not of models. The result will say more
   about the bets than about the leads, and §3 is written so that it can.
2. **The name is Snapback 4.** Siblings, not a sequence: "4" does not mean
   "after 3." A separate number gives the track its own corpus root,
   directory, package identity and data directory with no rename when the
   bakeoff resolves, and files never move in LLP. If Snapback 4 loses, it is
   a number that never shipped. "Snapback 3b" was considered and rejected:
   it reads as a variant of 3, and this is a different bet.
3. **Snapback 4 builds LLP 2000.000 as written.** Its §14 open questions are
   the lead's to decide inside the track, recorded in §7 as they are
   decided. The author rules on the bar, the comparison and promotion (§3).
4. **One owner on the core.** The lead writes the engine, the store and the
   client contract. Codex lanes build the fixture generator, the reference
   applications, the device witnesses and the periphery. Blind reviews from
   two model families are reserved for the two disclosure-sensitive
   mechanisms: partition inversion of read rules (LLP 2000.000 §7.1) and
   the meter's cut behaviour on non-uniform scans (LLP 2000.000 §6). This
   departs from the standing rule that Codex codes and Claude orchestrates,
   deliberately, for the core only.
5. **The clock is four weeks** from the first commit under `snapback4/` to
   the first slice running on the hub fixture. At the clock the author kills
   the track, merges its substrates into a survivor, or continues it. There
   is no third month without a ruling.
6. **Shared and not shared.** Shared with Snapback 3: the LLP corpus, the
   Clique harness and scorers, the fixtures, the briefs, and the simulator
   once either track has one. Not shared: code, except the Snapback 2 pieces
   both are expected to copy (LLP 2000.000 §5.1).
7. **Snapback 2 keeps shipping and freezes new substrate work for the
   duration.** Fix-forward, the loop bar, acquisition and papercuts continue;
   no new certificate axes, client custody mechanisms or ceiling changes.
   This is accepted here as a condition of the bakeoff. Making it binding on
   concurrent Snapback 2 sessions needs one line in `rules/NOT-DOING.md`'s
   Moves ledger, which this document does not write.
8. **Reading load.** This root, updated in place, and one append-only
   journal, `snapback4/JOURNAL.md`. Sub-LLPs only for a decision that must
   outlive the journal. No panels beyond decision 4's two reviews.

## 2. The bet on one page

**Keep verbatim from Snapback 2** (LLP 2000.000 §5.1): the rule language
with its uniformity and hoist analysis, `decided_true` and the
structural-fact refusals; one data language, Quarry, with `--` comments,
`require … else CODE`, `shape`, `merge`, `walk`, `chunk`; the value model
and its conformance corpus; typed read and write states with no silent state
and the refusal envelope `{code, family, site, message, rewrite, guide}`; the
successor classifier (widening additive, checked narrowing, exact projection
ownership); `deliver`'s engine-computed audience, tombstones and
`bootstrap`; `schedule`, `job`, `chunk` and rehearsal; personas; `init`,
`dev`, `check`, `doctor`, `run`, `test`, `why`, `trace`, `guide`; H0
`deploy`, `rollback`, `secret`; channels; assets; the word-prefix index.

**Flip 1 — certify authorization, meter resources** (LLP 2000.000 §6). The
certificate keeps every read site's shape, uniformity, completeness class
and prediction eligibility, and stops pricing bytes and steps. A
deterministic meter charges examined rows, index entries, rule probes,
steps, heap, output and rows written as the program runs, on the server and
in the client interpreter alike. A query cut by the meter returns `capped`
with no continuation past the cut, the same observable outcome as a static
ceiling cut today; a mutation aborts with `E_BOUND` naming actual work; a
job is chunked. `check --cost` prints the structural cost class; `test`
prints measured meters on the hub fixture.

**Flip 2 — the viewer's partition is the unit of sync** (LLP 2000.000 §7).
`sync to <audience> last N by <index>` on a table makes the engine maintain,
per principal, the rows that principal may read, as a change log with a
horizon; a table without the word is online-only, and `check` says which
rule term prevented inversion. The client holds a replica of its partitions,
one watermark each, a durable outbox with client-minted ids, and the
interpreter; queries run locally and are `complete` inside the horizon.
Revocation is a scope tombstone; `until-revoked` and `delivered-history`
survive as the client's leave policy. Two client tiers — online-only
without a store, local-first with one — share one language and one server.

**The vocabulary is fixed before the mechanism** (LLP 2000.000 §8):

```ts
type Read<T> = { data: T; complete: boolean; fresh: boolean; since?: number; next?: Cursor }
            | { loading: true } | { denied: Refusal };
row.pending: boolean;
type Write = { state: "pending" } | { state: "sent"; seq: number } | { state: "failed"; why: Refusal };
type Link = "online" | "offline" | "signed-out";
```

A screen that needs a word not on that card is a defect in the mechanism,
not a gap in the card.

**The hub comes first** (LLP 2000.000 §11): the Clique generator's `u0001`
with 1,000 memberships, `u0500` with 500 follows, and about 100,000
messages are the first fixture; day-two changes are designed before the
day-one screen; the native client is in slice one; physical floors are
measured before targets; the simulator is the primary harness.

## 3. The bakeoff protocol

Shared with Snapback 3; LLP 2000 or a sibling record should adopt the same
text so that the two tracks are measured by one instrument.

**Brief.** [`benchmarks/briefs/social-messenger.md`](../benchmarks/briefs/social-messenger.md)
(Clique) M1–M8, plus
[`benchmarks/briefs/messaging-day2-evolution.md`](../benchmarks/briefs/messaging-day2-evolution.md).
The field-work application (LLP 2000 §4) tests generality after the first
slice, on whichever tracks survive the clock.

**Fixture.** The Clique generator at the instrument freeze LLP 1023 and
LLP 1024 record (`f40559f37722c79178be664a11f8a6485f1508fd`): hub `u0001`
with 100 groups and 900 DMs, `u0500` with 500 follows, and the 100,015
messages of the retained 0.0.25 replay database. The same fixture for every
track and for the control.

**Harness and judge.** [`benchmarks/protocol.md`](../benchmarks/protocol.md);
the same three scorers as the 2026-09-05 run (gpt-6-astra, grok-4.6,
muse-spark); Convex as the control arm; published packages; fresh cold
agents with only the installed package and the task. Neither lead runs the
comparison or grades their own track. The author rules on the numbers.

**Decision rule, pre-registered.**

| Gate | Threshold | How measured |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Loop bar | Fresh agent, published package, reduced Clique M1–M3, ≤ 1,200 s, ≤ 1 Snapback-attributed correction loop, N ≥ 3 | The existing loop-bar protocol (`rules/NOT-DOING.md` §"The bars") |
| Hub acquisition | Full durable content plus first offline thread and search on `u0001`, within 2× the measured physical floor | Floors recorded in the journal in week 1 (§5); the same script on every track |
| Client bytes | Online tier ≤ Convex's shipped React client; the local-first tier's JavaScript ≤ PowerSync's shipped web client, with the wasm device measured beside it and its own budget set by the diet (§7, 2026-09-13: one implementation) | Measured on one pinned toolchain, recorded once in the journal, never assumed |
| Scenarios | LLP 2000 §4's six scenarios green under the simulator on the hub fixture; LLP 2000 §10's outcomes 1–5 and LLP 2000.000 §11's 6–7 | The simulator run record plus the device witnesses |
| Interaction budgets | LLP 2000 §7's targets | Recorded beside the gates; descriptive, not gating, in slice one |
| Holistic score | Three-scorer Clique score with the Convex control | Reported; descriptive, not gating |

# LLP 2000.000: Snapback 3 — keep the core, flip two substrates, start at hub scale

**Type:** RFC
**Status:** Draft
**Role:** Second opinion on LLP 2000; since 2026-09-12 also the design brief Snapback 4 builds (LLP 3000)
**Systems:** Snapback 3 product architecture, bounded computation, client data model, client API vocabulary, language and tooling, effects runtime, verification (simulation), performance budgets, process
**Author:** Claude Fable 5.1, at Charlie Cheever's request
**Date:** 2026-09-12
**Revised:** 2026-09-12 (adopted as the Snapback 4 design brief by LLP 3000 decision 3); 2026-09-12 (initial)
**Related:** [LLP 2000](./2000-snapback3.rfc.md) (parent), [LLP 3000](./3000-snapback4.explainer.md) (the Snapback 4 root that builds this brief), LLP 0000, [LLP 0329](./0329-agent-native-rebuild-diagnosis.research.md), [LLP 0329.014.004](./0329.014.004-bounded-data-programs-v1-design.rfc.md), [LLP 0329.016](./0329.016-agentic-dev-loop-from-scratch.rfc.md), [LLP 1000](./1000-snapback2.explainer.md), [LLP 1001](./1001-query-engine.spec.md), [LLP 1002](./1002-dev-loop.spec.md), [LLP 1012](./1012-local-first-v1.spec.md), [LLP 1018.003](./1018.003-truthful-and-composed-client.decision.md), [LLP 1018.008](./1018.008-elegance-after-the-corrections.research.md), [LLP 1018.008.007](./1018.008.007-affordable-offline-acquisition.research.md), [LLP 1018.008.008](./1018.008.008-locality-of-application-changes.research.md), [LLP 1018.008.009](./1018.008.009-residual-ergonomic-gaps.research.md), [LLP 1019](./1019-derived-delivery-custody.rfc.md), [LLP 1020](./1020-day-two-evolution-and-legible-ceilings.rfc.md), [LLP 1023](./1023-clique-builder-jump.rfc.md), [LLP 1024](./1024-single-lane-delivered-search.rfc.md), [LLP 1025](./1025-digest-final-emission-at-hub-scale.research.md), `rules/RULES.md`, `rules/NOT-DOING.md`, `benchmarks/runs/2026-09-05-clique-full-3x3/holistic-scores.md`

## 1. Summary

LLP 2000 is right that the whole application is the unit of design, right
that ownership comes before abstraction, and right to ask for deterministic
simulation and an early real deployment. Its weakness is that it treats the
two structural choices that produced Snapback 2's costs as open research
questions, and then proposes a first experiment at exactly the scale where
those costs are invisible. This document takes a position on both, names
what to keep from Snapback 2 verbatim, and rewrites the first slice so it
can fail for the right reasons.

The position, in one paragraph. Snapback 2's *core* is excellent and should
be copied: the rule language and its uniformity analysis, the structural-fact
family, one data language, typed states with no silence, refusals that paste
a rewrite, personas, the successor classifier, the derived per-recipient
log, the loop verbs and their sub-second budgets. Two *substrates* under
that core are where the record shows the hours went, and both should be
flipped rather than re-investigated: (1) the static certificate should keep
certifying **authorization and shape** and stop pricing **bytes and steps**,
which move to a deterministic runtime meter with honest `capped`/abort
outcomes; and (2) the client should hold **a replica of the viewer's declared
partition** — the local-first shape every shipped sync engine and Snapback
2's own `deliver` log converge on — instead of a per-query result cache
proven by receipts. Those two flips dissolve most of the machinery that
leaked into the application (receipts, coverage, halos, validators, grants,
ID slices, drains, `withHistory`, follower inboxes, per-table retention
words) and turn the 28-second acquisition into a bulk stream whose floor is
the device's write speed. The first slice should be built on the **hub
fixture** (1,000 memberships, 500 follows, 100,000 messages), with the
day-two change designed before the day-one screen, a native client in the
first slice, and a deterministic simulator as the primary test harness.

Three claims this document makes that LLP 2000 does not:

1. **The costs have two named causes, and both are decisions, not
   mysteries.** Byte-priced static certification and the receipt-proven
   result cache account for every ergonomic residual in LLP 1018.008.009
   except documentation. §2 shows the mapping.
2. **The first experiment must include the hub.** Snapback 2 was "elegant on
   day one and hostile on day two" (LLP 1020) and broke at 1,000 memberships
   (LLPs 1023, 1024, 1025). A two-principal slice will pass and teach nothing.
3. **The process is part of the product.** Seventy-one LLPs, over two
   thousand commits, and seven ceiling raises in fourteen days produced a
   sound system nobody can hold in their head. Snapback 3 needs one owner on
   the core, a simulator instead of a test matrix, and budgets anchored to
   competitors so that raising one means admitting a loss.

## 2. What the record says, read as causes

The evidence LLP 2000 §2 cites is right; its reading is too gentle. Every
row below is a Snapback 2 cost recorded in the corpus, with the substrate
that produced it. Numbers are the corpus's own and retain its scope limits.

| Recorded cost | Where | Cause |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Adding `editedAt: time ?` (44 bytes × 244 retained copies = 10,736) pushed `messages.searchAll` to 4,199,688 closure bytes against 4,194,304; the builder shrank a page from 20 to 19 | LLP 1018.008.008; 0.0.21 day-two diary | **Static byte pricing.** Closure bytes are a bound on what a receipt could ship; the product paid for the bound, not the work |
| The 1,000-membership digest fold certifies at 9,922,698 heap and 8,691,704 closure bytes and is refused; the recipient-keyed form is admitted at 2,733,253 / 1,848,418 | LLP 1025 §3 | **Static byte pricing** (heap = steps × 32 + closure scratch). A runtime meter would have run the fold and reported actual bytes |
| A message page is 50, not 200, because a row "prices at 16,016 bytes" and 200 exceed the 1 MiB output ceiling | LLP 1012 §3 | **Static byte pricing** of the worst-case row width |
| A per-recipient search projection is refused at 20,863,641 reserved steps against 1,000,000 | LLP 1024 §1 | **Static step pricing** of a derivation the engine would perform incrementally |
| Full acquisition of 10,008 messages: 67.4 s → 53.6 s → 32.6 s → 28.4 s against a 2–3 s objective; 201 requests, 200 acknowledgement mutations, 612 IndexedDB transactions; PowerSync reports 10,000 rows in 0.36 s native and 2.5 s on web | LLP 1018.008.007; LLP 1018.008.009 §4 | **Result cache, not replica.** Every 50-row page is a certified query, a receipt, a durable acknowledgement mutation, and a store swap |
| Adding a count to a thread makes the composite `partial` offline whenever count authority is missing, so the template declares an independent `history` query and `withHistory` bindings; 27 files / 1,718 lines → 28 / 1,751 after the fix | LLP 1018.008 F2; LLP 1018.008.009 §2 | **Result cache.** The count is not a fact the receipt covers, so the client cannot compose locally |
| `shared/thread.ts` describes table identity, row selection, order, create membership, draft fields and mutation targets a second time | LLP 1018.008 F2 | **Result cache.** Items must be reconstructed from answers because there is no local table to read |
| Sending stalled with 85 IDs remaining against a certified 352-ID slice on an 8,192-ID grant; recovery needed a "Sync now" action | LLP 1018.008 F1 | **Result cache + engine-minted IDs.** Prediction needs server ids before commit, so ids are granted in slices |
| A follower browser tab writes through a durable inbox row; the SDK carries `forwarding`, `writer-unresponsive`, `inbox-full`, `follower` states | LLP 1018.004 | **Result cache.** One writer per partition image; other tabs cannot mutate a receipt-proven image |
| Global search over 1,000 memberships costs ~1,010 HTTP requests through a client N-way merge | LLP 1024 §2 | **Result cache** (no local index over the partition) + **static pricing** (the server-side projection is refused) |
| The complete local runtime grew 7.8 KB → 89,843 gzip bytes; the ceiling was raised 34,000 → 50,000 → 66,000 → 82,000 → 82,500 → 84,000 → 85,000 → 90,000 | LLP 1000 §"Revised"; `rules/NOT-DOING.md` §Moves; LLP 1018.008 §10 | **Result cache.** The inputs list of `metrics/twin.jsonl` is coverage spans, extent facts, held directories, posting trees, fact integrity, swap journals — receipt machinery, not the interpreter |
| `Snapshot` carries `data, next, partial, receipt{state, source, seq, next, reason, rule, sites}, freshness{live, staleSince, connection}, local{predicted, retained, caughtUp, watermark}, refusal, status, reason` | LLP 1018.003 §1 | Both. Every field is a true fact about the mechanism; almost none is a fact about the application |
| `Schema::rule_hash` binds every queued write to the complete schema; an optional column on the subject table invalidates unrelated evidence | LLP 1018.002; LLP 1018.008.008 | **Result cache.** Evidence identity exists because held answers must be re-authorized; a replica re-runs the mutation at the server and needs no evidence identity |

Against that, what scored: on the 2026-09-05 three-scorer Clique run
Snapback 2 beat Convex on authorization (82 vs 79) and offline (81 vs 44)
and lost on queries and live (60 vs 85), client integration (59 vs 79),
setup and docs (55 vs 79), diagnostics and loop (66 vs 77), operations (53
vs 76) and scale (62 vs 80); overall 66 vs 81. The later single-lane 0.0.21
and 0.0.25 scores of 82 and 85 are real improvement and not a three-scorer
comparison. The wins are the core; the losses are composition, discovery,
scale and operations — the substrates and what sits on them.

LLP 2000 §2 summarises this as "costs arise where one layer must reconstruct
another layer's facts." True, and the layer doing the reconstructing is the
client, because it was designed as a cache that may not compute. Name the
cause and the remedy follows.

## 3. Where LLP 2000 is right, and should not be diluted

+    const TEXT: &str = "# Session\n\nA complete fact.\n\n```js\nrun();\n```\n";
+    fn root(label: &str) -> std::path::PathBuf {
+        let root = std::env::temp_dir().join(format!("guide1-{label}-{}", std::process::id()));
+        let _ = std::fs::remove_dir_all(&root);
+        std::fs::create_dir_all(&root).unwrap();
+        root
+    }
+    #[test]
+    fn inactivity_refresh_expiry_future_and_legacy_history() {
+        let root = root("expiry");
+        let read = |now| render_at(&root, "session", TEXT, false, 8000, now);
+        assert_eq!(read(1), TEXT);
+        let repeat = read(SESSION_MILLIS);
+        assert_eq!(repeat, TEXT);
+        assert!(!repeat.contains("--repeat") && !repeat.contains("already shown"));
+        assert_eq!(read(2 * SESSION_MILLIS), TEXT, "expiry uses the refreshed last read");
+        assert_eq!(read(1), TEXT, "future timestamps are invalid");
+        std::fs::write(root.join(".snapback4/guide-seen.json"), "[]").unwrap();
+        assert_eq!(read(2), TEXT, "legacy hash arrays start fresh");
+        std::fs::write(root.join(".snapback4/guide-seen.json"), "broken").unwrap();
+        assert_eq!(read(3), TEXT);
+        std::fs::remove_dir_all(root).unwrap();
+    }
+    #[test]
+    fn freshly_copied_history_never_suppresses_a_new_projects_read() {
+        let original = root("source"); let copied = root("copy");
+        render_at(&original, "session", TEXT, false, 8000, 100);
+        std::fs::create_dir_all(copied.join(".snapback4")).unwrap();
+        std::fs::copy(original.join(".snapback4/guide-seen.json"), copied.join(".snapback4/guide-seen.json")).unwrap();
+        assert_eq!(render_at(&copied, "session", TEXT, false, 8000, 101), TEXT);
+        let history = std::fs::read_to_string(copied.join(".snapback4/guide-seen.json")).unwrap();
+        assert!(!history.contains("complete fact") && !history.contains(copied.to_str().unwrap()));
+        std::fs::remove_dir_all(original).unwrap(); std::fs::remove_dir_all(copied).unwrap();
+    }
+    #[test]
+    fn concurrent_writes_are_atomic_and_never_hide_unemitted_content() {
+        let root = root("concurrent");
+        for _ in 0..12 {
+            let barrier = std::sync::Barrier::new(4);
+            std::thread::scope(|scope| {
+                let handles: Vec<_> = (0..4).map(|i| {
+                    let root = &root; let barrier = &barrier;
+                    scope.spawn(move || {
+                        barrier.wait();
+                        let text = format!("# Card {i}\n\nFact {i}.\n");
+                        let shown = render_at(root, &format!("card{i}"), &text, false, 8000, 100);
+                        assert!(shown.contains(&format!("Fact {i}.")));
+                    })
+                }).collect();
+                for handle in handles { handle.join().unwrap(); }
+            });
+            let h: History = serde_json::from_slice(&std::fs::read(root.join(".snapback4/guide-seen.json")).unwrap()).unwrap();
+            assert_eq!(h.last_read_ms, 100);
+            assert!(h.hashes.iter().all(|hash| hash.len() == 64));
+            assert_eq!(std::fs::read_dir(root.join(".snapback4")).unwrap().count(), 1);
+        }
+        std::fs::remove_dir_all(root).unwrap();
+    }
+}

 customer/staff/evidence/deadline/approval schema and on-demand feature sections.
 Its first read fits 8,000 UTF-8 bytes, a conservative 8,000 byte-token ceiling.
 Default card output has the same hard byte budget; references remain available
-with --full or an explicit section. Repeated project-local reads show only new

+with --full or an explicit section. Repeated project-local reads show new
+paragraphs or complete code fences within one reading session. GUIDE1 corrects
+TOK1's permanent history: a stored last-read Unix timestamp expires the hashes
+after 30 minutes of inactivity. Missing, malformed, legacy or future timestamps
+start fresh. A hash of the canonical project path invalidates history copied to
+a different project, even immediately; file mtime never establishes freshness.
+History stores only content/path hashes and the timestamp, never schema, account
+contents or the path itself. Writes atomically replace a process-and-counter
+unique temporary file; concurrent lost updates may reprint content, never mark
+unemitted facts seen. It is disposable and never affects check, security or execution.
+Changed content is shown again. This inactivity session cannot distinguish two
+readers using the same project within 30 minutes; it intentionally avoids new
+session flags or shell/environment identity requirements.
+
+A bounded selection with zero new blocks reprints its content with a one-line
+repeat note, without asking for another command. --repeat remains accepted.
+--full always prints the complete card up to 18,000 UTF-8 bytes, bypassing reading
+history. Above that bound it says plainly that the card is too long and lists
+sections. Evolution (~10.9 KB), day2 (~8.2 KB) and staging (~16.9 KB) motivated
+18,000 rather than 16,000: all three common bounded recipes fit. Deploy (~42 KB),
+language (~40 KB), rules (~34 KB), production (~28 KB), security (~52 KB) and
+rule-recipes (~100 KB) still page. Explicit sections use the same 18,000-byte
+bound, keeping one heading whole whenever it fits; only larger headings get
+continued parts. Default/first-read pages retain their 8,000-byte and line budgets.
+TOK6 package-reference manuals retain their 8,000-byte section pagination.

diff --git a/snapback4/packages/snapback4/reference/CHANGELOG.md b/snapback4/packages/snapback4/reference/CHANGELOG.md
index ac1db9a1cc..4c047d69b6 100644
--- a/snapback4/packages/snapback4/reference/CHANGELOG.md
+++ b/snapback4/packages/snapback4/reference/CHANGELOG.md
@@ -1,5 +1,11 @@
 # Changelog
 
+## 0.4.52
+
+### Guide changes
+
+- Guide reading history expires after 30 minutes of inactivity and resets when copied to another project. Reads with no new blocks reprint the bounded selection with a repeat note. `--full` prints the complete card up to 18,000 bytes; larger cards explain the limit and list sections. Explicit sections keep a heading together within that bound, including the persistent-production deploy recipe. Default and first-read budgets stay the same.
+
 ## 0.4.51
 
 - **Behavior change:** `createApp` with `webDevice()` now defaults to progressive startup. `app.ready` establishes a usable client and write custody, not an open/acquired replica; observe `localStatus` / `useLocalStatus` for local readiness. `startup: "awaited"` restores the old opening barrier. Openers without a custody probe (native/Expo) still await.

+test('GUIDE1: repeated CLI selections reprint, expiry and immediate project copies start fresh',()=>{
+ const cwd=mkdtempSync(join(root,'target/guide1-source-')),copy=mkdtempSync(join(root,'target/guide1-copy-'));
+ try{
+  mkdirSync(join(cwd,'snapback'));mkdirSync(join(copy,'snapback'));mkdirSync(join(copy,'.snapback4'));
+  const args=['guide','deploy','--section','15'];
+  const first=run(cwd,args),p=spawnSync(bin,args,{cwd,encoding:'utf8'});
+  assert.equal(p.status,0);assert.equal(p.stdout,first);assert.match(p.stderr,/^Repeat: /);
+  assert.doesNotMatch(p.stdout+p.stderr,/new: 0 blocks|--repeat/);
+  const file=join(cwd,'.snapback4/guide-seen.json'),history=JSON.parse(readFileSync(file));
+  writeFileSync(join(copy,'.snapback4/guide-seen.json'),JSON.stringify(history));
+  const copied=spawnSync(bin,args,{cwd:copy,encoding:'utf8'});assert.equal(copied.stdout,first);assert.equal(copied.stderr,'');
+  history.last_read_ms=Date.now()-31*60*1000;writeFileSync(file,JSON.stringify(history));
+  const expired=spawnSync(bin,args,{cwd,encoding:'utf8'});assert.equal(expired.stdout,first);assert.equal(expired.stderr,'');
+  assert.match(first,/Persistent production/i);assert.doesNotMatch(first,/continued/);
+  for(const name of ['evolution','day2','staging']){
+   const full=run(cwd,['guide',name,'--full']);assert.doesNotMatch(full,/too long to print whole|Truncated:/);assert.match(full,/```/);
+   assert.equal(run(cwd,['guide',name,'--full']),full,'full bypasses history');
+  }
+  for(const name of ['deploy','language','rules','production','security','rule-recipes'])assert.match(run(cwd,['guide',name,'--full']),/too long to print whole/);
+ }finally{rmSync(cwd,{recursive:true,force:true});rmSync(copy,{recursive:true,force:true});}
+});

+#[cfg(test)]
+mod session_tests {
+    use super::*;
+    const TEXT: &str = "# Session\n\nA complete fact.\n\n```js\nrun();\n```\n";
+    fn root(label: &str) -> std::path::PathBuf {
+        let root = std::env::temp_dir().join(format!("guide1-{label}-{}", std::process::id()));
+        let _ = std::fs::remove_dir_all(&root);
+        std::fs::create_dir_all(&root).unwrap();
+        root
+    }
+    #[test]
+    fn inactivity_refresh_expiry_future_and_legacy_history() {
+        let root = root("expiry");
+        let read = |now| render_at(&root, "session", TEXT, false, 8000, now);
+        assert_eq!(read(1), TEXT);
+        let repeat = read(SESSION_MILLIS);
+        assert_eq!(repeat, TEXT);
+        assert!(!repeat.contains("--repeat") && !repeat.contains("already shown"));
+        assert_eq!(read(2 * SESSION_MILLIS), TEXT, "expiry uses the refreshed last read");
+        assert_eq!(read(1), TEXT, "future timestamps are invalid");
+        std::fs::write(root.join(".snapback4/guide-seen.json"), "[]").unwrap();
+        assert_eq!(read(2), TEXT, "legacy hash arrays start fresh");
+        std::fs::write(root.join(".snapback4/guide-seen.json"), "broken").unwrap();
+        assert_eq!(read(3), TEXT);
+        std::fs::remove_dir_all(root).unwrap();
+    }
+    #[test]
+    fn freshly_copied_history_never_suppresses_a_new_projects_read() {
+        let original = root("source"); let copied = root("copy");
+        render_at(&original, "session", TEXT, false, 8000, 100);
+        std::fs::create_dir_all(copied.join(".snapback4")).unwrap();
+        std::fs::copy(original.join(".snapback4/guide-seen.json"), copied.join(".snapback4/guide-seen.json")).unwrap();
+        assert_eq!(render_at(&copied, "session", TEXT, false, 8000, 101), TEXT);
+        let history = std::fs::read_to_string(copied.join(".snapback4/guide-seen.json")).unwrap();
+        assert!(!history.contains("complete fact") && !history.contains(copied.to_str().unwrap()));
+        std::fs::remove_dir_all(original).unwrap(); std::fs::remove_dir_all(copied).unwrap();
+    }
+    #[test]
+    fn concurrent_writes_are_atomic_and_never_hide_unemitted_content() {
+        let root = root("concurrent");
+        for _ in 0..12 {
+            let barrier = std::sync::Barrier::new(4);
+            std::thread::scope(|scope| {
+                let handles: Vec<_> = (0..4).map(|i| {
+                    let root = &root; let barrier = &barrier;
+                    scope.spawn(move || {
+                        barrier.wait();
+                        let text = format!("# Card {i}\n\nFact {i}.\n");
+                        let shown = render_at(root, &format!("card{i}"), &text, false, 8000, 100);
+                        assert!(shown.contains(&format!("Fact {i}.")));
+                    })
+                }).collect();
+                for handle in handles { handle.join().unwrap(); }
+            });
+            let h: History = serde_json::from_slice(&std::fs::read(root.join(".snapback4/guide-seen.json")).unwrap()).unwrap();
+            assert_eq!(h.last_read_ms, 100);
+            assert!(h.hashes.iter().all(|hash| hash.len() == 64));
+            assert_eq!(std::fs::read_dir(root.join(".snapback4")).unwrap().count(), 1);
+        }
+        std::fs::remove_dir_all(root).unwrap();
+    }
+}

## 8. Wave-32 builder reading and waiting (TOK1)

The start card replaces the introductory card tour with one complete compiling
customer/staff/evidence/deadline/approval schema and on-demand feature sections.
Its first read fits 8,000 UTF-8 bytes, a conservative 8,000 byte-token ceiling.
Default card output has the same hard byte budget; references remain available
with --full or an explicit section. Repeated project-local reads show new
paragraphs or complete code fences within one reading session. GUIDE1 corrects
TOK1's permanent history: a stored last-read Unix timestamp expires the hashes
after 30 minutes of inactivity. Missing, malformed, legacy or future timestamps
start fresh. A hash of the canonical project path invalidates history copied to
a different project, even immediately; file mtime never establishes freshness.
History stores only content/path hashes and the timestamp, never schema, account
contents or the path itself. Writes atomically replace a process-and-counter
unique temporary file; concurrent lost updates may reprint content, never mark
unemitted facts seen. It is disposable and never affects check, security or execution.
Changed content is shown again. This inactivity session cannot distinguish two
readers using the same project within 30 minutes; it intentionally avoids new
session flags or shell/environment identity requirements.

A bounded selection with zero new blocks reprints its content with a one-line
repeat note, without asking for another command. --repeat remains accepted.
--full always prints the complete card up to 18,000 UTF-8 bytes, bypassing reading
history. Above that bound it says plainly that the card is too long and lists
sections. Evolution (~10.9 KB), day2 (~8.2 KB) and staging (~16.9 KB) motivated
18,000 rather than 16,000: all three common bounded recipes fit. Deploy (~42 KB),
language (~40 KB), rules (~34 KB), production (~28 KB), security (~52 KB) and
rule-recipes (~100 KB) still page. Explicit sections use the same 18,000-byte
bound, keeping one heading whole whenever it fits; only larger headings get
continued parts. Default/first-read pages retain their 8,000-byte and line budgets.
TOK6 package-reference manuals retain their 8,000-byte section pagination.

Build-time declaration digests for proof/client/auth derive from dist declarations,
with a generated API operation index derived from the bound schema. Digests are
bounded, identify omitted declarations, and are checked for declaration drift.
Every starter script points to the relevant digest. Init prints start before
starting an asynchronous frontend install with private progress/status files,
a join/status command and normal npm exit status. A request identity is published
before spawning, so delayed startup cannot join an earlier success. An IPC-owned
worker group stops npm and its process-group descendants if the installer is killed; progress
and custody files are private. A compact file custody map
and the generated API index reduce starter rereads.

Probe scripts announce their indefinite lifetime before deployment. A project
receipt records a held run's lifetime and request activity without credentials;
a previously terminated run held over 60 seconds with zero requests refuses
before deployment unless --hold or SNAPBACK4_PROBE_HOLD=1 is supplied.
Unattended foreground first runs also stop after a 60-second idle grace, with
bounded host cleanup and the probe:check recipe. Check-owned serveCheck runs set the hold override because their uptime is
already bounded by the checker. Explicit hold preserves the
harness's indefinite lifetime contract; the external bakeoff harness must pass that opt-in (SNAPBACK4_PROBE_HOLD=1).
This is a developer-wait safeguard, never an authorization or deployment gate.

Asset placement retains one home per asset (including distinct columns of the
same row), and placement by the uploader. E_ASSET_PLACED includes a concrete
same-bytes upload-per-column recipe. The uploads card demonstrates that recipe
and distinguishes shared references from separate placements. No security binder
or client contract changes are required. Evidence and independent reviews are
recorded with the workstream report; no publication or version bump is made.


TOK1 review records preserve every received result: [Muse round 1](reviews/3000.037.000-tok1-muse-round1.md)
and [fresh Codex round 1](reviews/3000.037.000-tok1-codex-round1.md) found
check-owned hold, cross-card context and installer custody/join/privacy issues;
these were repaired. [Muse round 2](reviews/3000.037.000-tok1-muse-round2.md)
was READY; [fresh Codex round 2](reviews/3000.037.000-tok1-codex-round2.md)
found late-init metadata preservation. Generated-leaf preflight and restoration
were added. The final start/initializer delta is READY from both
[Muse round 3](reviews/3000.037.000-tok1-muse-round3.md) and
[fresh Codex round 3](reviews/3000.037.000-tok1-codex-round3.md).

## 10. Serve lifetime and store recovery (SRV1)
use snapback4_lang::guide;

#[test]
fn honest_full_prints_small_cards_and_explains_large_ones() {
    for name in ["evolution", "day2", "staging"] {
        let card = guide::card(name).unwrap();
        assert!(card.len() <= guide::REFERENCE_BYTES, "{name}: {}", card.len());
        assert_eq!(guide::full(name, card), card);
    }
    for name in ["deploy", "language", "rules", "production", "security", "rule-recipes"] {
        let card = guide::card(name).unwrap();
        let shown = guide::full(name, card);
        assert!(shown.contains("too long to print whole"), "{name}");
        assert!(shown.contains("--section") && shown.len() <= guide::REFERENCE_BYTES);
    }
    let exact = "x".repeat(guide::REFERENCE_BYTES);
    assert_eq!(guide::full("edge", &exact), exact);
    assert!(guide::full("edge", &(exact + "x")).contains("too long"));
}

#[test]
fn deploy_persistent_production_is_one_complete_heading() {
    let raw = guide::card("deploy").unwrap();
    let card = guide::reader_card("deploy", raw);
    let sections = guide::sections(&card);
    let (_, heading, _, _) = &sections[14];
    assert!(heading.to_lowercase().contains("persistent"), "{heading}");
    assert!(!card.contains("persistent production (continued"));
    let shown = guide::section("deploy", &card, "15").unwrap();
    assert!(shown.len() <= guide::REFERENCE_BYTES);
    let (_, _, start, end) = guide::sections(raw).into_iter().find(|s| s.1 == *heading).unwrap();
    let recipe = raw.lines().skip(start).take(end - start).collect::<Vec<_>>().join("\n");
    for block in guide::reading_blocks(&recipe) {
        assert!(shown.contains(block.trim_end()), "missing recipe block: {block}");
    }
}

   221	            first,
   222	            "the first lookup is complete, including identical shapes of different operations"
   223	        );
   224	        let react = first
   225	            .replace(
   226	                "Plain: client.query(api.a, {}).",
   227	                "React: useQuery(api.a, {}).",
   228	            )
   229	            .replace(
   230	                "Plain: client.query(api.b, {}).",
   231	                "React: useQuery(api.b, {}).",
   232	            );
   233	        let delta = render_in(&root, "api react", &react, false);
   234	        assert!(!delta.contains("Args: {}."));
   235	        assert!(
   236	            delta.contains("## query a\n\nReact:") && delta.contains("## query b\n\nReact:"),
   237	            "{delta}"
   238	        );
   239	        let long = format!(
   240	            "# This app's client usage\n\n## query a\n\n{}\n\n{}\n\nTail: not yet emitted.\n",
   241	            "changed ".repeat(500),
   242	            "late ".repeat(770)
   243	        );
   244	        let clipped = render_in(&root, "api client", &long, false);
   245	        assert!(clipped.len() < snapback4_lang::guide::FULL_BYTES);
   246	        assert!(!clipped.contains("Tail: not yet emitted."));
   247	        {
   248	            assert!(
   249	                render_in(&root, "api client", &long, false).contains("Tail: not yet emitted.")
   250	            );
   251	        }
   252	        let single = format!(
   253	            "# This app's client usage\n\n## query a\n\n{}\n",
   254	            "oversized ".repeat(790)
   255	        );
   256	        assert_eq!(
   257	            render_in(&root, "api client", &single, false),
   258	            single,
   259	            "a fitting fact is never starved by the delta prefix"
   260	        );
   261	        assert_eq!(render_in(&root, "api react", &react, true), react);
   262	        std::fs::remove_dir_all(root).unwrap();
   263	    }
   264	}

   267	mod session_tests {
   268	    use super::*;

   270	    fn root(label: &str) -> std::path::PathBuf {
     1	//! Bounded reading sections; the compiled corpus retains complete examples.
     2	// @ref LLP 3000.037.000#8 — preserve one heading per explicit section within the reference bound.
     3	use super::{FULL_BYTES, REFERENCE_BYTES, reading_blocks};
     4	
     5	/// Flatten nested reference headings and split at complete paragraphs/fences.
     6	/// Long runnable references stay in the package, behind a file pointer.
     7	pub fn reader_card(name: &str, card: &str) -> String {
     8	    if card.len() <= FULL_BYTES { return card.into(); }
     9	    let title = card.lines().next().unwrap_or("# Guide");
    10	    // Package manuals keep TOK6's existing bounded pagination contract.
    11	    let limit = if name.starts_with("package-reference ") { FULL_BYTES } else { REFERENCE_BYTES };
    12	    let room = limit.saturating_sub(title.len() + 350);
    13	    let mut out = format!("{title}\n\nRead one section for your change. `guide {name} --sections` lists sections.\n\n");
    14	    let mut heading = "Opening".to_owned();
    15	    let mut page = String::new();
    16	    let mut part = 1;
    17	    let flush = |out: &mut String, page: &mut String, heading: &str, part: usize| {
    18	        if page.is_empty() { return; }
    19	        out.push_str(&format!("## {heading}{}\n\n{}\n", if part == 1 { String::new() } else { format!(" (continued {part})") }, page));
    20	        page.clear();
    21	    };
    22	    let mut fenced = false;
    23	    // Isolate headings even where source prose has no preceding blank line.
    24	    let mut prepared = String::new();
    25	    for line in card.lines().skip(1) {
    26	        if line.starts_with("```") {
    27	            if !fenced { prepared.push('\n'); }
    28	            fenced = !fenced;
    29	        }
    30	        if !fenced && (line.starts_with("## ") || line.starts_with("### ")) { prepared.push('\n'); }
    31	        prepared.push_str(line); prepared.push('\n');
    32	        if !fenced && (line.starts_with("## ") || line.starts_with("### ") || line.starts_with("```")) { prepared.push('\n'); }
    33	    }
    34	    for block in reading_blocks(&prepared) {
    35	        if block.starts_with("## ") || block.starts_with("### ") {
    36	            flush(&mut out, &mut page, &heading, part);
    37	            heading = block.trim().trim_start_matches('#').trim().to_owned(); part = 1;
    38	            continue;
    39	        }
    40	        let block = if block.len() > room && block.starts_with("```") {
    41	            // These complete sources are packed by pack-recipes.mjs.
    42	            let reference=match name {
    43	                "testing" if block.contains("day2-accounts")=>"day2-accounts.mjs",
    44	                "social" if block.contains("// src/Conversation.tsx")=>"social/Conversation.tsx",
    45	                "social" if block.contains("// src/useJumpToMessage.ts")=>"social/useJumpToMessage.ts",
    46	                _=>panic!("oversized guide fence needs its own packed reference: {name}"),
    47	            };
    48	            format!("Complete reference: node_modules/snapback4/dist/recipes/{reference}. Read only this file when adapting that example.\n")
    49	        } else { block };
    50	        // Dense prose can exceed a section; split only prose at word boundaries.
    51	        let chunks = if block.len() > room {
    52	            let mut chunks = Vec::new(); let mut chunk = String::new();
    53	            for word in block.split_inclusive(char::is_whitespace) {
    54	                if chunk.len() + word.len() > room && !chunk.is_empty() { chunks.push(std::mem::take(&mut chunk)); }
    55	                chunk.push_str(word);
    56	            }
    57	            if !chunk.is_empty() { chunks.push(chunk); } chunks
    58	        } else { vec![block] };
    59	        for chunk in chunks {
    60	            if page.len() + chunk.len() + 1 > room { flush(&mut out, &mut page, &heading, part); part += 1; }
    61	            page.push_str(&chunk); page.push('\n');
    62	        }
    63	    }
    64	    flush(&mut out, &mut page, &heading, part);
    65	    out
    66	}

snapback4/crates/snapback4-lang/src/guide/receipts.rs:138:Sends predict in ordinary history with `pending: true`, after the highest ordinal held for that group and in this device's submission order. Settlement replaces provisional positions with server ordinals, so rows can move. The confirmed counter stays unchanged; provisional recipient counts are zero and never enter accounting. Scope creation and joins predict with held authority and coverage, including the creator's membership. Missing acquisition/authority or an exhausted safe-integer position queues without a row. Existing membership updates/removals, delivery, compaction and seen queue without prediction to preserve settled accounting. Generated progress excludes pending messages; member/progress return null for a pending membership, and projections return null for pending messages and exclude pending recipients. The SDK never collects provisional rows as delivery. Pending metadata uses the ordinary existence classifier (guide client). At compile time, a mutation that reads clause-owned or derived accounting becomes `predicts: never` and queues without a row. `check` prints one `N_RECEIPT_PREDICTION` note at the first dependency; `why <op>` explains it. An explicit `predicts:` assertion refuses with `E_NOT_PREDICTABLE`. The rule follows helpers, aliases, loops and all branches, including untaken branches. It includes receipt builtins; owned-field keys, filters and write targets; maintained aggregates and parent columns; whole-row builtin arguments, comparisons, collections and explicit returns. Ordinary sends, creates, joins, stable-id edits and reaction upserts still predict when they read only clean fields such as id/text. A receipt-derived unread count does not stop those sends: device predictions skip its maintenance. Its stored value remains the last settled value, and pending inserts, edits and deletes never enter a query-time recount. While its parent group has predicted source or member writes, a row-keyed count returns that settled value with `recountPending: true` and `complete: false`; affected ordinary aggregate groups also become incomplete. This is a settled snapshot, not an optimistic unread estimate or a lower bound. A pending new member has no count until settlement. The pending check uses the base and current overlay images, including deletions and both groups of a move. It caches O(pending) point reads per target/query; other groups keep their live counts and expiry. Deletes save ordinary group keys in the durable overlay, so an insert then delete still names its group after restart. Only a computed/receipt-derived group key, missing group field, or legacy no-image overlay without saved keys forces a table-wide fallback. An unpinned scan is incomplete if any group is dirty, but clean returned rows still recount normally. The answer flags do not distinguish a frozen snapshot from incomplete live counting, especially after scalar projection. Render every incomplete receipt-derived badge as a stable number with a quiet refreshing indication, never `L+`. After settlement, the ordinary live-count/expiry and coverage rules resume. A parent-key total computed only on read has no settled cache and returns null/incomplete while its source is pending. Server-maintained parent columns keep their existing unmarked synchronized-value semantics. Reading any of these values inside a mutation still makes it `predicts: never`, even before the write or in a later mutation. Receipt-derived sums remain server-only because they can overflow; target constraints and updates that can move a row-keyed member's count domain also prevent prediction. Member writes keep the member and source-group authority precondition; source writes do not run that member-write check. Both keep their ordinary write authorization. Deferred row-keyed maintenance keeps its full public reservation even for an online-only target, including `fanout × 4` written rows for a source write, and checks the recipient cap using only parent/expiry index keys. The compiler exports complete per-source `source_reservations`, calculated by the same functions the server uses, including ordinary private maintains in schemas without receipts. A device skipping receipt-derived or online-only public aggregate work pays the shared server worst-case reservation, including on insert; an ordinary server insert may charge less actual work. Receipt allocation pays a fixed 51-key allowance; successful inserts also pay for the server counter replacement without writing or reading a guessed counter on the device. Receipt-derived sums and counter scopes with additional data-dependent hooks make their writers server-only. Ordinary online-only aggregates keep predicting within their fixed reservation. Ordinary synced aggregates still execute their shared delta path. Private formulas and totals stay off the device. Every predicting source write also charges declared image/video list slots, even for null or empty cells, without taking the asset ledger: per slot, 2 rows written, 4 examined, 8 steps and 16,384 transient heap bytes. The implicit result of a final insert/update is the pending write image, not an authored whole-row read. Use a stable message id to predict an edit. Allocation has one runtime refusal guard: after touching provisional allocation, any error rolls back the overlay and keeps the intent queued. Receipt schemas without compiler classification also queue. Field names are static; arbitrary forged compiler metadata is outside the backend contract. No per-value taint or count-path traversal runs. A kept backend without the new capabilities opens with only affected programs downgraded: receipt-source writes, hidden effects, existing receipt readers and writes whose complete price cannot be recovered queue. Unrelated fully priced posts or conversation-title edits still predict offline under Kept. An old private-column projection without prices cannot identify hidden source effects, so every unpriced table writer conservatively queues, including unrelated profile saves and posts. This affects both Clique calibration apps. Supplying the bundled backend while offline does not replace Kept; prediction resumes at the first online adoption of that backend. With no private-column owner markers anywhere, the structural zero proof keeps unrelated zero-cost writers predicting. Outbox ids, arguments and delivery debt survive; see `guide offline-interactions` for the upgrade note. Replaying a write id allocates nothing twice. Reports assert the recipient's

snapback4/crates/snapback4-lang/src/guide/deploy.md:14:For Postgres, prefer `SNAPBACK4_STORE_URL` with explicit schema/ha=none and Unix sockets; a password in `--store-url` is visible in process arguments. Serve requires `--worker-state-dir /absolute/durable/path` or `SNAPBACK4_WORKER_STATE_DIR`. Queries, identified writes, sessions, ephemeral state and ordinary maintained count/sum columns are admitted. A deploy may add, redefine or drop those columns and indexes on them; it atomically recounts sources and mirrors parents before publishing the generation. Ordinary releases may also add tables, add optional or literal-defaulted columns, add/drop/redefine indexes, and retire ordinary tables or columns into dormant storage. Use `deploy DIR --restore-dormant` to restore retired names and their exact rows/values. A restored column must repeat its retained declaration: changing its type, default, text constraints or deletion policy refuses just as an in-place edit would. Unique collisions refuse the whole activation. Ordinary Postgres schema evolution currently refuses `evolution-authority` when the schema contains a durable temporal, read-through or recorded table, including an unchanged one. Their activation-time source retirement needs the later lifetime slice. Previously admitted maintained-column transitions with unchanged ordinary declarations remain available. Existing column types/defaults, rules, audiences and other authority declarations must stay unchanged. Type narrowing, backfills, renames, temporal/read-through/recorded evolution and retirement of tables with maintained columns remain named refusals. Token minting (`token`), SQL/data and release inspection, rollback, previews, prune, staging, saved versions and hot activation refuse; excluded runners, certified sync and complete configuration: `guide release-json`. SDK guest/signup/login and proof seedAccounts retry explicit identity 429 refusals using retryAfterMs (Retry-After for older servers). Sign-in has at most four attempts within timeoutMs (default three minutes); signal cancels requests/waits. No lost or malformed reply is replayed. Time-gated mutations on the real clock need a bounded retry of the authored refusal (for example `NOT_OPEN`), with a deadline and short delay; never retry unrelated refusals. Development and classified staging proofs can advance the clock (`guide time`); production always uses wall time.
snapback4/crates/snapback4-lang/src/guide/deploy.md:29:Keep the session and send `Authorization: Bearer <token>`. Wrong credentials or a disabled identity method are 401 E_AUTH. A signed-in account deletes itself with `POST /auth/delete` (bearer; `app.auth.deleteAccount()` or `deleteAccount(url, token)` from `snapback4/auth`): every session, the credential and the principal go, the email can sign up again, the app's rows stay for a cleanup mutation or effect. An operator deletes one with a service token: `POST /auth/delete-account {"principal":"user:…"}`. Where the data lives: the deployment directory (`store.sqlite`, `releases/`); hosting it is `guide production --section hosting`. Sessions survive ordinary deployments and restarts until expiry/revocation; a replaced/reset store requires sign-in again. Never delete a store to make adoption pass. `serve --port N` accepts 0 or A-B; stdout's first line is its URL, stderr includes SNAPBACK4_READY. Wait for readiness. Layout: releases/N/ holds source; store.sqlite data/sessions/queues; assets/ uploads. The database owns current release/history; current is a text convenience copy, releases.jsonl a convenience ledger. Back up the whole directory, including sidecars/assets/releases, with serve and external workers stopped (guide package-reference client §6). A restore does not rewind external effects.
snapback4/crates/snapback4-lang/src/guide/deploy.md:57:This roster is synced. A whole service-seat branch may add the first member; ordinary callers must already hold the roster. Service grants add no device audience. The seat's own program lookups and `exists` obey read rules too: to inspect a roster, add `or viewer = 'service:ops'` to its read rule, as above. A rule probe has authorization visibility; a program probe does not. The mutation completes before the grant syncs; wait for the enrolled account's client to receive its scope before using offline staff reads. A synced row has at most one group-roster audience: cross-table crew grants are not a second group. Enroll those readers in the same roster or keep the table `online only` (`guide rule-recipes`). Mint locally against the same store. Token stdout is only the credential; prose is stderr. Protect it; it expires under the seat-session policy. Rotate the credential by revoking its session (guide services) and minting a replacement. Expired or revoked bearer sessions cannot invoke the mutation. A repeat grant hits uniqueness; add membership revocation deliberately. Programmatic account sessions use `mintSession(url, serviceToken, principal)` from `snapback4/worker` (guide services); service seats themselves are minted locally by `token`.
snapback4/crates/snapback4-lang/src/guide/deploy.md:72:Preview rollback after a v2 edit, forward after a v1 edit, and v2-native rows without a v1 preimage. A bridge keeps current value and old workflow until authorized repair. `prune` discards migration preimages and dormant data, names blinded `down` projections, and can make reversal/re-entry refuse; keep history or a backup.
snapback4/crates/snapback4-lang/src/guide/deploy.md:79:`deploy ./deployment --check` previews deploy direction and restoration intent. `bunx snapback4 check --store ./deployment/store.sqlite --release 1` also previews a selection; `bunx snapback4 releases ./deployment latest` inspects it. Forward with `bunx snapback4 rollback ./deployment --to latest`. Preview runs exact adoption on a private disk snapshot and removes it, without activating; disk/time scales with the store, and later writes can change the plan. Release is immutable source; latest is the highest successfully adopted source even after rollback. A refused deploy removes its candidate directory and private stage. An ambiguous reply preserves source until committed history resolves the outcome; inspect releases before retrying. Bare rollback selects the previously active release; rollback prints the resolved target before preview or activation. Generation is a monotone identity; releases N/latest inspects source. Source identical to an active ordinary release is a no-op; a deploy equal to an ordinary release selects that release when its rollback view is live. source matching a retained release reactivates its existing id with restoration and full validation. Deploy/rollback return after serve switches: `release N is current as generation G`. In-flight one-shot operations finish in their generation; no settle sleep is needed. Default adoption is one measured receipt; --verbose/--json includes phases/cache advice. N_ADOPTION_PAUSE appears above one second. Deploy/rollback wait up to five seconds for a shutting-down store owner before admission; live serve keeps its authenticated control path. A remaining owner refuses with E_STORE_BUSY; stop that owned process before retrying. Failed or ambiguous activation is never automatically replayed. `await host.stop()` from startServe waits for its native child and the kernel store lock, including delayed worker cleanup, before resolving. `snapback4 stop ./deployment` asks the retained owner to shut down through its same-owner private control socket and waits for the store lock; it never signals a recorded PID or removes a lock. `serve ./deployment --wait-stopped` remains the bounded five-second lock barrier after a caller has already requested shutdown; it neither stops a process nor opens SQLite. It refuses other serve flags. A timeout rejects cleanup rather than claiming the store is free. Widened enum values, or short codes a loose release wrote, become dormant rows when selected types cannot hold them; compatible forward restores exact values. Reference dependents also leave the active population. Rules, counts, maintained views and reacquired sync partitions see only active rows. Point reads/updates/ deletes see absent rows; dormant ids stay reserved. Nominal reference values stay legal, as for other missing ids; require/read-through decides live-target admission. Dormant rows occupy no cap/unique slots, so older writes can require a bridge repair. Removed tables/columns/indexes also stay dormant. Fresh-source name reuse needs --restore-dormant; selecting retained source by deploy or rollback already selects restoration. The same --restore-dormant flag is accepted by both commands. Old active updates keep dormant cells; active deletion removes them. Only active referential actions run: parent deletion invalidates dormant lifetimes. Narrowed rows with invalid visible references stay dormant; whole-table/column restoration retains its E_ADOPT barrier. Prune previews counts/bytes/sample ids; `prune ./deployment --allow-destructive` permanently drops all dormant storage. No age collector or implicit pruning exists. Deploy/rollback --allow-destructive bypasses neither validation nor retention. --allow-feed-adoption permits the explicit feed launch. Inspect releases after an ambiguous reply. Details: guide package-reference server — Release lifecycle (`guide package-reference server --section release lifecycle`).
snapback4/crates/snapback4-lang/src/guide/deploy.md:101:Password accounts, ids, data, retained assets and release/rollback history survive. Users sign in again: inherited full/read/service tokens and refresh families are revoked, and devices reacquire under a fresh store identity. `promotion.json` records the source and new incarnations with release and clock provenance. Mint destination operator credentials with the ordinary local `token` command. E_PROMOTE refuses existing/nested targets and live sources. Finish queued work and started external steps before stopping; promotion refuses them, live guest sessions that cannot sign in again, paused/advanced staging clocks and production security findings. Use real-time staging for promotion; test-clock timestamps are never rebased. `--check` runs the same private-copy qualification without publishing.
snapback4/crates/snapback4-lang/src/guide/deploy.md:118:These dormant-row outcomes require `rollback <deployment> --to 1 --exact`. A registered default view keeps widened old-table rows visible with the current guard. Ordinary renames, required-field additions and splits with a fitting down do not require a view merely because the app declares an invariant. A non-each extraction registers a fallback view for future independent children. When carrying current guards leaves the old tables and programs unchanged, no current independent intent needs retaining, and a private reverse-activation preview fits without hiding old rows, default rollback selects the original source and result shape. Otherwise it selects the registered view; live activation repeats validation. A needed view uses the current workflow or a compatible loosened bridge with current guards. A guarded current view names removed nullable fields as absent results and removed writes as E_OP; author a compatible current endpoint to keep an old write available. On a pre-0.4.39 store, a refusal names `rollback <deployment> --to N --exact` and "redeploy with 0.4.39 to register a view"; preview exact selection with --check before accepting its historical behavior. Absent operations still return E_OP. Test audiences and guards with real accounts. Rollback previews lead with records hidden from tables the target release has, then empty/defaulted legacy cells and newer-only table rows. Counts precede read rules; they do not infer birth release or a viewer’s query result. Declare a reverse projection for a truthful old representation, or keep a bridge. Guard/refusal and query/count/safety diffs follow. Verify filtered/paged/per-viewer results and denominators. Literal-default backfills print `table.column ← value on N rows` in previews and cold/hot deploy receipts: interpreted historical fields, not physical writes. Preview shows old and new rule clauses and mutation `require`s side by side, preserving Boolean parentheses. A proved tightening is a note, including `require A else X` becoming `require A else X; require B else Y` when A's refusal contract is unchanged; changed codes/messages still warn. A loosening or unproved implication is a WARNING, including rollback that removes a new guard. Unchanged clauses and consulted keys, fields, rules, auth and visibility are silent. Unread column domains and forward domain widening do not unprove a probe; narrowing still gets exact dormant-value/population warnings. Changed dependencies group once: verify refusal cases. Comparison never changes admission. Missing operations/schedules, populated removed tables, changed clauses and job bodies are named separately. An unchanged job compiling differently after a dependency change gets a note. Predicate-only read-through changes preserve bound captures and parent lifetimes; structural capture changes and new uncaptured rows still need safe audience correspondence. Receipts and previews count storage by kind: `1 row, 2 cells, 0 index entries dormant`. Dormant rows cover their retained cells; standalone cell counts/samples belong only to active rows. Bytes/assets follow the same disjoint ownership. Samples are labelled and unique within each kind/path, for example `rows notes [A]; cells notes.color [B, C]`. A retained index definition represents logical entries to rebuild, not a dormant physical index. A widening note describes values later writes could use; a compatibility WARNING counts only current values the selected release cannot hold. Compatible forward has no such warning.
snapback4/crates/snapback4-lang/src/guide/deploy.md:129:The new versions/v1 captures selected modules, their contemporary fixtures, an importable `versions/v1/generated/api.ts` plus its package marker, and `versions/v1/release.json` with the actual immutable release id, source hash and CLI version. An unchanged deploy keeps its id and may refresh those derived generated/label artifacts only when the saved directory's complete Quarry source set and bytes exactly match the selected source; source and fixture evidence stay intact, and differing source refuses. Choose a new positive-numbered versions/vN for changed source; saving cannot combine with --check. Saved-source deploy uses its own fixtures and emits the saved source's API rather than the working tree's API. Use the recorded id when choosing a baseline; since_release remains the author-supplied newer-schema label. The bridge prefers the complete current guarded workflow, retaining new tables and current rules; this app's `bridge/ROLLBACK-CHECKLIST.md` names old-client domain/input and safety-clause repairs. Retain current domains, audiences, guards and quotas; repair records through authorized mutations; prove blocked cases refuse and affected lists/counts remain correct; repeat the old-source preview. No bridge is activated or business migration synthesized. JSON includes checklist; --verbose retains full evidence/index paths. Dormant rows reserve ids but no unique slots: new active rows may block restoration. A widened non-unique bridge permits repair before reinstating uniqueness; retained storage cannot supply absent safety logic.
snapback4/crates/snapback4-lang/src/guide/deploy.md:131:Hot deploy and rollback require the running `serve` host to support this CLI's activation protocol, including `--exact` selection of original source. snapback4/crates/snapback4-lang/src/guide/deploy.md:153:A table declares `table observations renamed from behaviors:` to preserve its row/reference identity and old-release writes; it refuses merging independent table histories. It never supplies ordinary mutation-time file validation. Active writes refresh accepted backfill inputs; older-release edits still need recomputation agreement or conflict repair. An absent optional rename destination is not a conflict.
snapback4/crates/snapback4-lang/src/guide/deploy.md:274:Storage, users and rows survive. Resolve reported conflicts through a truthful bridge. Ordinary check policy belongs to SEC7. The recorded test result includes its age; stale or missing evidence is explicit. Without a matching completed campaign they demand `snapback4 test` for the selected source and fixtures (saved-source previews name `test --source`). Add `--strict` to refuse reachable refuted intent before activation; unproved intent and no-invariant notes remain advisory. A finite campaign is evidence, not proof. snapback4/crates/snapback4-lang/src/guide/feeds.md:53:Planned `home` scans global time indexes and filters by follows; it is not a guaranteed complete-history reader. Unrelated posts consume work. Sparse history can exhaust the 16 MiB heap or 8,192-examination meter: `complete: false`, no `next`, never a complete empty page. An inbox cursor cannot continue this different reader. Starting below the inbox's oldest event can skip uncopied events from another author. Use the generated `useFollowing` reader below for complete online Following history; the profile reader is for one actor.
snapback4/crates/snapback4-lang/src/guide/feeds.md:203:### Exact online history

The hook owns preparation, retries, revision restarts, event identity, live prepend and withdrawal; call its `loadMore` when `hasMore`. Do not splice an inbox cursor into history or use `usePage` for this reader.
snapback4/crates/snapback4-lang/src/guide/feeds.md:209:`phase` is `preparing`, `page`, `stalled`, `restart`, `refused`, or `offline`. Preparing is not empty. Render `items`; report `omissions` (including `E_FOLLOWING_ROW_TOO_LARGE`) and a refusal's `code`. Recent deliveries may appear live before the next session; displayed events remain until withdrawal or reset. Later births (including backdated arrivals) and new-follow history are covered by the next session: call the hook's `refresh()` to start it. The live window alone does not cover arrivals missed between polls. Unfollow/removal and card authorization withdraw mounted items. Replay pins card bytes; edits do not rewrite historical pages. Expiry restarts explicitly. Four independent sessions per viewer/view are allowed; `E_FOLLOWING_QUOTA` never evicts another tab. `retry` retries a refusal after its cause is resolved.
snapback4/crates/snapback4-lang/src/guide/feeds.md:211:Offline the hook uses the unchanged inbox and reports `fact`: “older posts are not on this device”. Its `inbox` read retains the ordinary loading/denial state; that state is not evidence of an empty feed. Devices never build history heads from source floors. Pending own-post and own-edge fences precede server pages. `check --cost` prints a separate Following history certificate; the ordinary inbox certificate still describes the query. Supported bindings are one-for-one card projections over a descending Following page of 1–100 events, with scalar fields and ordinary non-expiring unique point reads.
snapback4/crates/snapback4-lang/src/guide/feeds.md:213:`useFollowing` requires a full session: read-only clients and read bearers refuse `E_READ_ONLY` without creating history custody. Application follows may use any public residency; the feed uses its private owned edges.

snapback4/crates/snapback4-lang/src/guide/witness.md:76:separate 900-second deadline for explicit `native:smoke` and `witness ios --build`. Automatic background preparation allows 900 seconds without new phase output or log growth, up to 60 minutes total. This measures output activity, not semantic build advancement: repeated retries or heartbeat lines can keep a non-advancing build and its private simulator occupied until the 60-minute cap. This is the accepted bound for CocoaPods/Xcode output; silent stalls still fail with the build diagnostic; `preparation-deadline.json` records the progress budget. Node launcher resolution gets at most 30 seconds (or a shorter --timeout), with cancellation. Finish any pinned-Node download in the project before retrying; smoke keeps its 120-second step default.

snapback4/crates/snapback4-lang/src/guide/witness.md:128:`status` reports the current publication as pending, passed, skipped, failed or unverified. It and `doctor` compare the published generation and Hermes hash with the latest proof; a missing or stale handoff reports no proof for the current export, including equal-byte re-exports and failed skip markers. `status`/`doctor` observe a consistent generation under a nonblocking shared publication lock; a commit in progress reports pending. `--wait` follows that transaction through verification and handoff, retrying changed observations for up to five seconds; a stable missing handoff stays unverified. `--wait` follows the current publication and returns 0 only for its passed proof (1 failure, 2 pending/skip/no proof). Older superseded results remain in their own run directories. A failed proof includes its log path. Doctor presents native status as information; use `status --wait` for the native-proof exit code. Background QoS lowers native work's scheduling priority; shared simulator services and I/O mean it is not a zero-contention guarantee. After maintenance quiesces, five full plus twenty compressed runs remain. Current-publication evidence stays full regardless of start order; active exports and pending publication transactions also retain their evidence. Retention runs inside native owner-death process custody with a 30-second work deadline followed by the existing bounded group drain. Every committed automatic export schedules maintenance, including a failed status handoff, after publication releases its lock and without waiting for maintenance. Requests coalesce into one pending slot beyond an active custodian. Busy requests record pending and return; one background observer probes for availability without starting a native work group. Unfinished or leased discoveries remain pending until covered; the observer waits for publication/run/export leases and live owners at a 100 ms probe interval instead of repeatedly starting workers. Every retention wait is on a live lease holder or birth-matched owner, and is bounded. The passive observer and its follow-up chain have one 60-minute deadline, including the launch gate; expiry leaves a loud pending request for a later export. Ownerless publication transactions recover automatically under their leases. Run initialization is leased through export-lease acquisition and journalling, with a five-second refusal bound for a stuck initializer. Retention claims per-run custody under that lease and releases it before recovery or deletion. Ownerless unfinished runs are repaired by a bounded scan: current evidence stays full, while empty or partial abandoned shells are removed and do not consume keep slots. Conflicting identities or persistent maintenance errors park with a diagnostic rather than spinning. Pending records are fully written before atomic publication; a scan keeps a durable claim until successful completion, and acknowledgement cannot erase a newer request. Legacy partial claims are repaired idempotently. A finishing custodian remains the observer's backup until a live successor scan has claimed pending work; a busy queue lock is not a handoff acknowledgement. Every export first inspects the actual full/compressed evidence, publication pins and live owners, and re-arms excess, stranded or parked maintenance even if no request was ever written and its Expo export later fails. Loss of both peers, a crash before a peer exists, or a parked error is retried by the next export; this is not a host daemon. The pending observer and active custodian supervise each other during a scan: if either dies, the survivor resumes the claim after native custody drains, without another export. Three interrupted attempts park with a diagnostic; a fresh export may retry. Known work errors park rather than retrying themselves. Readiness probes an exclusive publication lease, so shared holders also wait passively. Failed Expo wrappers remove their own private attempts under their lease; stale journals are classified by owner liveness, and empty failed attempts never occupy history slots. A claimed scan gets the 30-second work budget; proof-bound maintenance still defers immediately on a busy lock. Queue records and maintenance.log live directly under artifacts/native-smoke/. Both pre-driver and post-driver retention failures defer maintenance without failing the proof. Orphan deletion runs after releasing the publication lock. The public `witness export-retain` alias uses this same supervised wrapper; its internal worker requires recorded native custody. Cancellation and sleep cancel retention; newer publication also cancels proof-bound retention. A stalled compressor cannot hold the proof slot indefinitely. `native:smoke` always builds and waits; a cache miss in automatic smoke builds in the background.

snapback4/crates/snapback4-lang/src/guide/release-json.md:116:Row-keyed view-recount remains refused. Ordinary releases can add tables, optional/literal-defaulted columns and indexes, redefine/drop indexes, and retire ordinary tables/columns into dormant storage. `deploy DIR --restore-dormant` restores those names, rows, values and reference lifetimes atomically. Ordinary Postgres schema evolution currently refuses `evolution-authority` when the schema contains a durable temporal, read-through or recorded table, including an unchanged one. Their activation-time source retirement needs the later lifetime slice. Previously admitted maintained-column transitions with unchanged ordinary declarations remain available. Ordinary column types/bounds, requiredness, defaults and text/cap constraints can change, including when restoring a retained definition. Incompatible values become dormant together with transitive references to them; later compatible widening restores them without coercion. New required or context-defaulted columns need existing rows to supply their values: an empty table succeeds, while missing historical values refuse `E_ADOPT`. Contextual defaults never invent history. Unique/cap failures roll back the activation, including hiding/restoration. Authority declarations stay unchanged; retirement/restoration of tables carrying temporal/read-through/recorded or maintained authority waits for a later slice.

snapback4/crates/snapback4-lang/src/guide/rules.md:83:`sync` infers exactly the parent's substituted audience after service grants are removed. A conjunction such as membership and an archived flag stays `online only`; `check --verbose` explains its server reads. A parent-pinned page resolves authorization once, while completeness still requires the child's own coverage. An unknown or mismatched parent takes the server fallback. Retained history stays marked `retained: true` beside the server's current page. On these syncing delivered-history children, `retained` is row metadata; name an application column `wasRetained` instead. Row shapes and spreads preserve the mark.
snapback4/crates/snapback4-lang/src/guide/rules.md:187:Each of `read`, `insert`, `update` and `delete` takes one rule over the row (`.col`), `viewer` and literals. A table without a rule for an effect denies it. `public 'why'` on its own line is the read rule. The compiler supplies indexes for `exists` probes; named equalities may cover a total unique key and check extra columns on that one row, for both `exists` and `created`, without an extra index. A full positional unique key may come first, followed by named extra-column tests, as in `docs[.docId, ownerId = viewer]`. Every focal field used by the key or extra tests must be pinned for one verdict to serve a whole page. `created t[..]` is valid in insert rules only. A rule cannot vary with time (use `expire`). A read rule that is exactly `exists t[.a, .b, viewer]`, `.col = viewer` or `public` can be synced (guide sync). The system principal consults no rules, so `system` is never a rule word. An audience read rule may add `or viewer = 'service:pusher'` (or several service grants); sync infers its audience without them, and ordinary extra disjuncts still refuse.
