    21	/// The section number (1-based, as the index numbers it) and heading whose
    22	/// words start with `heading`'s: how an alias's section is found.
    23	fn numbered_heading(card: &str, heading: &str) -> Option<(usize, String)> {
    24	    let wanted = words_of(heading);
    25	    sections(card).into_iter().enumerate().find(|(_, s)| words_of(&s.1).starts_with(&wanted)).map(|(n, s)| (n + 1, s.1))

    28	/// `guide <alias>` for an alias that names a section of `card` (the card
    29	/// `name`, as printed): the title, a line saying where this is and how to
    30	/// read the rest, then the section. `None` for an alias of a whole card.
    31	pub fn alias_landing(word: &str, name: &str, card: &str) -> Option<String> {
    32	    let (target, heading) = super::alias_section(word)?;
    33	    if target != name { return None; }

    39	        word.trim(), lines[start..end].join("\n").trim_end()))
    40	}

    43	/// holds: the section it names, or the card's opening for a whole-card alias.
    44	fn alias_inside(name: &str, card: &str, words: &str) -> Option<String> {

    50	        words.trim(), lines[..opening_end(card, &found)].join("\n").trim_end()))
    51	}

    53	fn distance(a: &str, b: &str) -> usize {
    54	    let b: Vec<char> = b.chars().collect();
    55	    let mut row: Vec<usize> = (0..=b.len()).collect();
    56	    for (i, a) in a.chars().enumerate() {
    57	        let mut diagonal = row[0];
    58	        row[0] = i + 1;
    59	        for (j, b) in b.iter().enumerate() {
    60	            let old = row[j + 1];
    61	            row[j + 1] = (diagonal + usize::from(a != *b)).min(row[j] + 1).min(old + 1);
    62	            diagonal = old;
    63	        }
    64	    }
    65	    row[b.len()]
    66	}

    68	/// Rank names and aliases first, then exact words in card contents, then typos.
    69	pub fn nearest(word: &str) -> Vec<&'static str> {
    70	    let word = word.to_lowercase();
    71	    let score = |term: &str| (!term.contains(&word) && !word.contains(term), distance(&word, term));
    72	    let mut cards: Vec<_> = KEYWORDS.iter().map(|&card| {
    73	        let best = ALIASES.iter().filter(|(_, target)| *target == card).map(|(alias, _)| score(alias)).chain([score(card)]).min().unwrap();
    74	        let in_card = super::card(card).is_some_and(|text| text.split(|c: char| !c.is_alphanumeric()).any(|token| token.eq_ignore_ascii_case(&word)));
    75	        let rank = if !best.0 { 0 } else if in_card { 1 } else { 2 };
    76	        ((rank, in_card && card != "language", best.1), card)
    77	    }).collect();
    78	    cards.sort_unstable();
    79	    let mut nearest: Vec<_> = cards.into_iter().take(3).map(|(_, card)| card).collect();
    80	    if super::LANGUAGE.split(|c: char| !c.is_alphanumeric()).any(|token| token.eq_ignore_ascii_case(&word)) && !nearest.contains(&"language") {
    81	        nearest[2] = "language";
    82	    }
    83	    nearest
    84	}

    86	// @ref LLP 3000.000.000#21 — extraction widening and guarded rollback use the reviewed release contract.
    87	pub(super) const EVOLUTION: &str = r##"# Evolution: adopting a schema
    88	
    89	Schema adoption applies a new backend definition to existing data.
    90	The stored rows must fit the new types, keys and constraints.
    91	
    92	```quarry
    93	table notes:
    94	  body: text <=200
    95	  archived: bool default false  -- fills this field in older rows
    96	  label: text <=40 ?            -- older rows read null
    97	  read <- .owner = viewer
    98	  owner: principal
    99	```
   100	
   101	New optional columns, required columns with literal defaults, non-unique
   102	indexes, tables and operations adopt without ceremony. Literal defaults
   103	fill older row images on the server and device, including in new indexes,
   104	without rewriting stored rows or logging partition events.
   105	
   106	Required columns without literal defaults, tighter types, unique indexes
   107	and stronger constraints need the existing rows to fit. A new required
   108	`default now` or `default viewer` column needs an empty table.
   109	
   110	## Operations
   111	
   112	Run `snapback4 check` against the dev store, or `check --store <path>`:
   113	each failed successor step is E_ADOPT at its column, index or table, with
   114	a rewrite. `dev` refuses that step on save and keeps the previous generation.
   115	A green check means the current rows fit; a later write can change that.
   116	Without a store, check only compiles.
   117	
   118	## Release rollback and hot activation
   119	
   120	`deploy <dir>` and `rollback <dir> [--to N]` activate atomically while `serve`
   121	is running. Rollback keeps removed durable tables, columns and index definitions
   122	as dormant storage, invisible to old programs and sync. Forward restores their
   123	values. Older updates preserve dormant cells; deletion removes them. New older-
   124	release rows receive the forward literal default or optional null. A required
   125	field can declare a conditional/derived `backfill` in the candidate schema;
   126	deploy previews exact transformed-row counts and commits the patch with the
   127	release. Keep declarations across later releases; rollback restores untouched
   128	preimages or names a conflict, and forward catches up older writes. Contextual
   129	defaults never invent history. `guide deploy` contains the complete persistent
   130	production recipe. REL5 owns release selection and compatibility narration.
   131	
   132	An incompatible enum/text/type value becomes a dormant row during release activation,
   133	with its original values restored on compatible forward. Dev adoption still refuses
   134	incompatible values with E_ADOPT. Restored uniqueness
   135	and parent-lifetime/audience checks can also refuse; failure keeps the old release.
   136	`--allow-destructive` never bypasses these checks.
   137	
   138	Reusing dormant names with new source requires `deploy <dir> --restore-dormant`;
   139	`rollback --to N` explicitly selects an immutable release and restoration.
   140	`prune <dir>` previews counts/bytes/ids; `prune <dir> --allow-destructive`
   141	permanently removes all dormant storage. Assets stay retained but unreadable
   142	while dormant. `releases <dir>` inspects database-authoritative history.
   143	
   144	Clients reacquire a fresh generation for changed execution content, including
   145	native-only module changes. A failed hot adoption leaves the old release serving.
   185	repair source and checklist. Preview before selecting it.
   186	
   187	An ordinary extracted payload may widen required → optional while retaining its
   188	underlying kind, bounds and lifetime; reversal still validates the old required
   189	payload. Keep its read policy: a compatible stricter child policy needs no
   190	intermediate bridge release. Missing bytes, other incompatible type changes or
   191	broader read audiences refuse atomically. Image and bounded asset-list columns
   192	use the same ordinary extraction form.
   193	
   194	### One record per photo in a list
   195	
   196	For a historical `albums.photos: [image <=10000] <=8`, declare the complete
   197	new source below. The `each` modifier moves one element into each child;
   198	plain `extract` moves a whole list into one child. Element types must match.
   199	
   200	```quarry
   201	use identity password
   202	table albums:
   203	  owner: principal
   204	  immutable owner
   205	  read <- .owner = viewer
   206	  delete <- .owner = viewer
   207	  online only
   208	table albumPhotos:
   209	  album: albums on delete cascade
   210	  photo: image <=10000
   211	  immutable album
   212	  cap 8 by album
   213	  read through .album
   214	  online only
   215	extract albumPhotos from albums via album each: {photo:photos}
   216	mutation removeAlbum(album: albums):
   217	  delete albums[album]
   218	```
   219	
   220	Child IDs stay stable across rollback/forward and old-client reordering.
   221	Reverse selection collects current child photos, including native v2 children,
   222	in receipt order with native children appended by ID. It moves their homes
   223	back into the old list; it never truncates an old list to its declared bound.
   224	An unrepresentable population becomes dormant under the ordinary old-domain
   225	rules. Empty lists create no children. Repeated asset IDs refuse because one
   226	asset cannot have two homes. Missing bytes, independent destination history,
   227	pruned children, invalid parent lifetimes and broader audiences refuse atomically.
   228	If populated children are dormant under a narrowed child domain, reverse
   229	selection refuses rather than omitting their photos. Restore those rows with
   230	a loosened child-domain bridge first; migration-owned null removals stay dormant.
   231	A temporarily retired child table resumes its validated current homes under the
   232	same extraction; it never replays consumed lists or recaptures independent children.
   233	
   234	This is `guide evolution`; client development commands are in guide package-reference client §5."##;
   235	
   236	/// A card's `##`/`###` headings outside code fences: (level, heading, first line, end line).
   237	pub fn sections(card: &str) -> Vec<(usize, String, usize, usize)> {
   238	    let lines: Vec<&str> = card.lines().collect();
   239	    let mut fenced = false;
   240	    let mut found: Vec<(usize, String, usize, usize)> = Vec::new();
   241	    for (index, line) in lines.iter().enumerate() {
   242	        if line.trim_start().starts_with("```") { fenced = !fenced; continue; }
   243	        if fenced { continue; }
   244	        let level = line.chars().take_while(|c| *c == '#').count();
   245	        if !(2..=3).contains(&level) || !line[level..].starts_with(' ') { continue; }
   246	        found.push((level, line[level..].trim().to_owned(), index, lines.len()));
   247	    }
   248	    // A section runs to the next heading at its level or above.
   249	    for i in 0..found.len() {
   250	        let level = found[i].0;
   251	        if let Some(next) = found[i + 1..].iter().find(|next| next.0 <= level) { found[i].3 = next.2; }
   252	    }
   253	    found
   254	}

   262	/// Dense cards with very long lines need the same bounded first read.
   263	pub const FULL_BYTES: usize = 8_000;
   264	/// Explicit reads fit evolution (~11 KB), day2 (~8 KB) and staging (~17 KB).

   267	pub const REFERENCE_BYTES: usize = 18_000;

   269	/// Explicit --full always reprints a complete card or explains why it must page.

   272	    format!("guide {name}: too long to print whole ({} bytes; --full limit {REFERENCE_BYTES} bytes). Read one section with --section <number or words>.\n\n{}", card.len(), section_index(name, &super::reader_card(name, card)))
   273	}
   274	
   275	/// Bound explicit selections separately from first/default reads.

   278	}

   280	const OPENING_LINES: usize = 40;

   282	/// Lowercase words, punctuation and backticks dropped: `sync to` == "Sync to".
   283	fn words_of(text: &str) -> String {
   284	    text.chars().map(|c| if c.is_alphanumeric() { c.to_ascii_lowercase() } else { ' ' }).collect::<String>().split_whitespace().collect::<Vec<_>>().join(" ")
   285	}

   287	/// Where the opening (the text before the first `##`) ends.
   288	fn opening_end(card: &str, found: &[(usize, String, usize, usize)]) -> usize {
   289	    found.first().map(|s| s.2).unwrap_or_else(|| card.lines().count())
   290	}
   291	
   292	fn index_lines(card: &str, found: &[(usize, String, usize, usize)]) -> String {
   293	    let opening = opening_end(card, found);
   294	    let mut out = String::new();
   295	    if opening > 5 { out.push_str(&format!("    0. Opening ({opening} lines)\n")); }
   296	    for (n, (level, heading, start, end)) in found.iter().enumerate() {
   297	        out.push_str(&format!("{}{:>2}. {heading} ({} lines)\n", "   ".repeat(level - 1), n + 1, end - start));
   298	    }
   299	    out
   300	}

   411	/// The innermost section holding a line: its number (0 for the opening) and heading.
   412	fn section_of(found: &[(usize, String, usize, usize)], line: usize) -> (usize, &str) {
   413	    found.iter().enumerate().filter(|(_, s)| s.2 <= line && line < s.3).max_by_key(|(_, s)| s.0)
   414	        .map(|(n, s)| (n + 1, s.1.as_str())).unwrap_or((0, "Opening"))
   415	}

   417	/// The alternatives of a search: `a|b` and `a OR b`.
   418	fn alternatives(words: &str) -> Vec<String> {
   419	    words.split('|').flat_map(|part| part.split(" OR ")).map(|alternative| alternative.trim().to_owned()).filter(|alternative| !alternative.is_empty()).collect()
   420	}
   421	
   422	/// A word as written, and its singular (`loops` finds "loop", `queries` "query").
   423	fn forms(word: &str) -> Vec<String> {
   424	    let mut forms = vec![word.to_owned()];
   425	    if let Some(stem) = word.strip_suffix("ies").filter(|stem| stem.len() > 2) { forms.push(format!("{stem}y")); }
   426	    else if let Some(stem) = word.strip_suffix('s').filter(|stem| stem.len() > 2 && !stem.ends_with('s')) { forms.push(stem.to_owned()); }
   427	    forms
   428	}
   429	
   430	// @ref LLP 3000.007#20.1 — `--find a b` is every word on one line; `a|b` and `a OR b` are either.
   431	/// Line numbers (0-based) whose text holds an alternative: its phrase, or
   432	/// every one of its words (each as written or singular).
   433	fn mentions(card: &str, words: &str) -> Vec<usize> {
   434	    let lines: Vec<String> = card.lines().map(str::to_lowercase).collect();
   435	    let mut found = Vec::new();
   436	    for alternative in alternatives(words) {
   437	        let phrase = alternative.to_lowercase();
   438	        let each: Vec<Vec<String>> = words_of(&phrase).split_whitespace().map(forms).collect();
   439	        found.extend((0..lines.len()).filter(|&i| {
   440	            if lines[i].contains(&phrase) { return true; }
   441	            let line = words_of(&lines[i]);
   442	            !each.is_empty() && each.iter().all(|forms| forms.iter().any(|w| line.contains(w.as_str())))
   443	        }));
   444	    }
   445	    found.sort_unstable();
   446	    found.dedup();
   447	    found
   448	}
   449	
   450	/// Lines holding any one of several words (connectives dropped), for a
   451	/// search whose words no single line holds together.
   452	fn any_mentions(card: &str, words: &str) -> Vec<usize> {
   453	    if alternatives(words).len() != 1 { return Vec::new(); }
   454	    let each: Vec<&str> = words.split_whitespace().filter(|w| !["or", "and", "OR", "AND"].contains(w)).collect();
   455	    if each.len() < 2 { return Vec::new(); }
   456	    mentions(card, &each.join("|"))
   457	}
   458	
   459	/// `guide <card> --section <words>`: the title line and one section, found by
   460	/// its number in the index (`0` or `opening` for the text before the first
   461	/// heading), its heading (any case, punctuation ignored), a unique prefix, a
   462	/// unique substring, or headings holding every word. When no heading matches
   463	/// but the words occur, the section holding most of their mentions prints, or
   464	/// every mention when they spread; only words no line mentions refuse, naming
   465	/// the nearest heading by spelling.
   466	pub fn section(name: &str, card: &str, words: &str) -> Result<String, String> {
   467	    let found = sections(card);
   468	    let lines: Vec<&str> = card.lines().collect();
   469	    let title = lines.first().filter(|line| line.starts_with("# ")).map(|line| format!("{line}\n\n")).unwrap_or_default();
   470	    let wanted = words_of(words);
   471	    let print = |start: usize, end: usize| format!("{title}{}", lines[start..end].join("\n").trim_end());
   472	    let opening = opening_end(card, &found);
   473	    if matches!(wanted.as_str(), "0" | "opening" | "intro" | "introduction") {
   474	        if let Some((_,heading,start,end))=found.first() {
   475	            if heading == "Opening" {return Ok(print(*start,*end));}
   476	        }
   477	        return Ok(lines[..opening].join("\n").trim_end().to_owned());
   478	    }
   479	    if let Ok(n) = wanted.parse::<usize>() {
   480	        return match found.get(n.wrapping_sub(1)) {
   481	            Some((_, _, start, end)) => Ok(print(*start, *end)),
   482	            // `1000` is an alias of the bounds card, not its thousandth section.
   483	            None => alias_inside(name, card, words).ok_or_else(|| format!("guide {name} has no section {n}\n{}", section_index(name, card))),
   484	        };
   485	    }
   486	    // An alias that names a section of this card lands there (`subpaths` would match two headings).
   487	    if super::alias_section(words).is_some_and(|(target, _)| target == name) {
   488	        if let Some(landing) = alias_inside(name, card, words) { return Ok(landing); }
   489	    }
   490	    let numbered: Vec<(usize, &(usize, String, usize, usize), String)> = found.iter().enumerate().map(|(n, s)| (n + 1, s, words_of(&s.1))).collect();
   491	    let stages: [&dyn Fn(&str) -> bool; 4] = [
   492	        &|heading| heading == wanted,
   493	        &|heading| heading.starts_with(&wanted),
   494	        &|heading| heading.contains(&wanted),
   495	        &|heading| wanted.split(' ').all(|w| heading.split(' ').any(|h| h.starts_with(w))),
   496	    ];
   497	    for stage in stages {
   498	        if wanted.is_empty() { break; }
   499	        let matches: Vec<_> = numbered.iter().filter(|(_, _, heading)| stage(heading)).collect();
   500	        match matches.as_slice() {
   501	            [] => continue,
   502	            [(_, (_, _, start, end), _)] => return Ok(print(*start, *end)),
   503	            many => return Err(format!("{words:?} matches {} sections of guide {name}: {}; pick one by number: snapback4 guide {name} --section {}", many.len(), many.iter().map(|(n, s, _)| format!("{n}. {}", s.1)).collect::<Vec<_>>().join(" · "), many[0].0)),
   504	        }
   505	    }
   506	    // No heading matches. A curated alias naming a section of another card
   507	    // lands there, saying so (`guide wire --section acceptance`).
   508	    // @ref LLP 3000.007#20.1 — acceptance under wire, statements under language, loops under writes.
   509	    let hits = mentions(card, words);
   510	    if let Some((target, _)) = super::alias_section(words).filter(|(target, _)| *target != name) {
   511	        if let Some(landing) = super::card(target).and_then(|text| alias_landing(words, target, text)) {
   512	            let here = match hits.len() {
   513	                0 => String::new(),
   514	                1 => format!(" (one line of guide {name} mentions it: --find {words:?})"),
   515	                n => format!(" ({n} lines of guide {name} mention it: --find {words:?})"),
   595	    for (start, end) in ranges {
   596	        if printed >= FIND_LINES { break; }
   597	        let (n, heading) = section_of(&found, start.max(hits.iter().copied().find(|&h| h >= start).unwrap_or(start)));
   598	        if n != current { out.push_str(&format!("{n}. {heading}\n")); current = n; } else { out.push_str("  …\n"); }
   599	        for (i, line) in lines.iter().enumerate().take(end).skip(start) {
   600	            let hit = hits.binary_search(&i).is_ok();
   601	            out.push_str(&format!("{:>5}{} {line}\n", i + 1, if hit { ':' } else { ' ' }));
   602	            printed += 1;
   603	            shown += usize::from(hit);
   604	        }
   605	    }
   606	    if shown < hits.len() { out.push_str(&format!("… {} more; narrow the words or read one section with --section <number>\n", hits.len() - shown)); }
   607	    out.push_str(&format!("Whole sections: snapback4 guide {name} --section <number>"));
   608	    // Search rows are numbered excerpts, not executable fences. Keep the query
   609	    // and the first actual matches when a dense result exceeds the read budget;
   610	    // paragraph-only bounding would discard the entire result as one block.
   611	    if out.len() > FULL_BYTES {
   612	        let tail=format!("\n… more matching context; narrow the words or read snapback4 guide {name} --section <number>");
   613	        let room=FULL_BYTES.saturating_sub(tail.len());
   614	        let end=out[..out.floor_char_boundary(room)].rfind('\n').unwrap_or_else(||out.floor_char_boundary(room));
   615	        out.truncate(end);out.push_str(&tail);
   616	    }
   617	    if any { Err(out) } else { Ok(out) }
   618	}
   619	
   620	
   621	/// Paragraphs and complete fences are the units of reading and project deltas.
   622	// @ref LLP 3000.037.000#8 — never cut a runnable fence in half.
   623	pub fn reading_blocks(text: &str) -> Vec<String> {
   624	    let mut blocks = Vec::new(); let mut block = String::new(); let mut fenced = false;
   625	    for line in text.lines() {
   626	        if line.trim_start().starts_with("```") { fenced = !fenced; }
   627	        if line.is_empty() && !fenced {
   628	            if !block.is_empty() { blocks.push(std::mem::take(&mut block)); }
   629	        } else { block.push_str(line); block.push('\n'); }
   630	    }
   631	    if !block.is_empty() { blocks.push(block); } blocks
   632	}
   633	
   634	/// Conservative byte-token ceiling: any UTF-8 byte needs at most one byte token.
   635	pub fn bounded_default(name: &str, text: &str) -> String {
   636	    bounded(name, text, FULL_BYTES)
   637	}
   638	
   639	fn bounded(name: &str, text: &str, limit: usize) -> String {
   640	    if text.len() <= limit { return text.to_owned(); }
   641	    let hint = format!("\nTruncated: selection budget is {limit} bytes. New question: snapback4 guide {name} --section <words>; section list: --sections.\n");
   642	    let mut out = String::new();
   643	    for block in reading_blocks(text) {
   644	        if out.len() + block.len() + hint.len() + 1 > limit { break; }
   645	        out.push_str(&block); out.push('\n');
   646	    }
   647	    out.push_str(&hint); out
   648	}
//! Long references are compiled into the CLI, never installed as whole manuals.
// @ref LLP 3000.037.000#18-bounded-package-reference-and-wiring-lookups-tok6 — topic/section access replaces discoverable manual files.
pub const TOPICS: &[(&str, &str)] = &[
    (
        "server",
        include_str!("../../../../packages/snapback4/reference/SERVER.md"),
    ),
    (
        "client",
        include_str!("../../../../packages/snapback4/reference/CLIENT-AND-OPERATIONS.md"),
    ),
    (
        "following",
        include_str!("../../../../packages/snapback4/reference/FOLLOWING-ADOPTION.md"),
    ),
    (
        "offline-uploads",
        include_str!("../../../../packages/snapback4/reference/OFFLINE-UPLOADS.md"),
    ),
    (
        "changes",
        include_str!("../../../../packages/snapback4/reference/CHANGELOG.md"),
    ),
];

pub fn card(topic: &str) -> Option<String> {
    let (_, raw) = TOPICS.iter().find(|(name, _)| *name == topic)?;
    // History's empty parent headings hold the version. Keep each version's
    // bullets together rather than flattening its repeated Guide changes child.
    let history;
    let raw = if topic == "changes" {
        history = raw
            .lines()
            .filter(|line| !line.starts_with("### "))
            .collect::<Vec<_>>()
            .join("\n");
        history.as_str()
    } else {
        raw
    };
    Some(super::reader_card(
        &format!("package-reference {topic}"),
        raw,
    ))
}

# Rules

**Snapback 2 optimizes the edit-to-verified loop — the agent's building an app on it, and ours building it.**

Speed is the constraint. Everything below exists to keep the edit-to-verified loop short
and to stop this repository accreting apparatus faster than anyone can remove it. These
rules bind `snapback2/` and the LLPs numbered 1000 and up; Snapback 1 is baselined, not
exempt, and its numbers may only fall.

Each rule is **[check]** (enforced by `caps`) or **[review]** (enforced by a human).

## Budgets — every budget is a trade, not a limit

- **5 blocking checks, 60s total.** A sixth requires deleting one, in the same PR. **[check]**
- **59 documents in the working set** (`llp/current/`). Today's count. A 60th requires
  archiving one — which is just removing the link. Snapback 2 aims to leave this at 15;
  Snapback 1's links are archived at promotion. **[check]**
- **12 documents in the foundation** (`llp/foundation/`). **[check]**
- **This file: 700 words.** If it grows, something becomes a check or stops being a rule. **[check]**
- **1,500 lines per source file, baseline 204.** Generated files exempt — and generated
  files are built, not committed; so are benchmark run records under `benchmarks/runs/`,
  which are evidence, not source. The baseline is Snapback 1's oversize count on the day
  the rule landed; it may not grow, and `snapback2/` contributes zero to it. **[check]**

## Blocking checks

`caps` · `ref-check` · `snapback2/scripts/test.sh` · `snapback2/scripts/measure.mjs --check` · `check-inventories`

## Loop shape

- **Every check reports all failures in one run.** Fail-fast is banned. **[check]**
- **Nothing blocks on anything slower than 60 seconds.** Slow verification runs on a
  schedule, attributed to the breaking commit, and files a bounded set of issues. **[review]**
- **Fix loops get 3 rounds.** Then stop: escalate to a human or descope. **[review]**

## Time budgets

Tracked every run of `snapback2/scripts/measure.mjs` (appends `snapback2/metrics/loop.jsonl`).
A regression is a P0 with a name on it.

| | budget |
|---|---|
| Incremental rebuild after a one-line edit | 15s |
| Test what you just changed | 30s |
| Blocking gate suite | 60s |
| Full build, warm cache | 5 min |
| **`snapback2 check` on a program (the agent's product latency)** | **1s** |
| **`snapback2 doctor` boot-to-ready** | **0.5s** |

## Scope

- **`NOT-DOING.md` is binding.** Moving something onto the doing-list means writing why
  and taking something off. **[review]**
- **Delete; don't deprecate.** No compat shims or migration paths before promotion. **[review]**
- **Nothing is compiled, transpiled, or generated at runtime that could be built ahead of
  time.** **[review]**
- **A spec needs an implementer and a date, or it isn't written.** **[review]**
- **Only implemented specs bind.** A document with no running code behind it is a
  proposal, and a proposal cannot block a PR or justify a check. **[review]**

## Agents

- **Agents remove apparatus freely and add none.** An agent PR that adds a check, script,
  registry, config file, or design doc needs explicit human approval saying so. **[review]**
- **2026-09-06 session exception:** the user delegated the search implementation
  decisions; one `snapback2-core/build.rs` may generate pinned Unicode tables at
  build time, alongside the existing package build. No new gate, registry,
  runtime generator, or Unicode dependency is authorized by this exception. **[review]**
- **Snapback 2 LLPs are numbered 1000 and up**; `llp-create` must be told. **[review]**
- **Snapback 4 LLPs are numbered 3000 and up** (`snapback4/`, LLP 3000); it adopts these budgets
  and loop shape as its own and keeps its scope in `snapback4/NOT-DOING.md`. **[review]**

## Shared machines

- Warm, named lanes, one per stream of work. Never clone fresh; reuse the worktree.
- **Never `git stash`.** Use a temporary commit.
- Kill only PIDs you recorded at launch. Never by name or pattern.
# LLP 3000: Snapback 4 — the decided-substrates candidate

**Type:** Explainer
**Status:** Active
**Role:** Root of the Snapback 4 corpus (LLPs 3000 and up)
**Systems:** Everything under `snapback4/` once it exists; the bakeoff protocol shared with Snapback 3 (LLP 2000) and Snapback 2 (LLP 1000)
**Author:** Charlie Cheever / Claude Fable 5.1
**Date:** 2026-09-12
**Revised:** 2026-10-29 (Integration 34: disposable proof caches and fresh-store fixture custody); 2026-10-27 (SQW: bound empty-cycle bulk probes independently of commit notifications); 2026-10-24 (RCPU bounded short-queue capacity); 2026-10-23 (§7: G4a bounded short admission, current worker/queue constants and original deadlines); 2026-10-23 (author view-once start-time contract, §7); 2026-10-22 (K0-Q fix round 1: reserve view-once consumption before byte delivery under bounded committer admission); 2026-10-13 (FLAKE: isolate real-timer client fixtures and keep quote layout pooled); 2026-10-13 (§7: timed-out compiler-group inspector retries within the unchanged drain deadline); 2026-10-12 (§7: a reference declares what deleting its row does, LLP 3000.039); 2026-10-12 (§7: `file` assets, LLP 3000.038); 2026-10-08 (§7: S3 long-wait capacity — sized viewer and claim pools, per-principal share, `E_STREAMS`, shared change answers, per-ticket engine wakeups); 2026-10-08 (§7: FL asynchronous initial identity capture and independent owned cleanup); 2026-10-07 (§7: FL fault dispatch checks exact birth and command); 2026-10-06 (§7: PD stale-enumeration regression distinguishes Darwin identity rereads); ## Summary

Snapback 4 is one of two parallel candidates for the successor to Snapback 2,
and the sibling of Snapback 3, not its successor. It builds
[LLP 2000.000](./2000.000-keep-the-core-flip-two-substrates.rfc.md) as
written: the Snapback 2 core kept verbatim, two substrates flipped up front —
resources metered at runtime instead of priced statically, and a client that
replicates the viewer's declared partition instead of caching receipted
results — proved first on the hub fixture, with a deterministic simulator as
the primary harness. Snapback 3 ([LLP 2000](./2000-snapback3.rfc.md), led by
Codex / GPT-6 Astra) keeps the same two questions open and discovers their
answers in its first slice. Snapback 2 ([LLP 1000](./1000-snapback2.explainer.md))
continues as the incumbent and the control. All three are judged by one
pre-registered comparison on one clock.

This document records the author's decisions of 2026-09-12 that created the
track, restates the bet on one page so a reader can orient without the brief,
fixes the bakeoff protocol, draws the fence, and plans the first four weeks.
The design argument and its evidence stay in LLP 2000.000. Only running code
will bind (`rules/RULES.md`).

## 1. Decisions (author, 2026-09-12)

On 2026-09-12 Charlie Cheever asked whether Claude Fable 5.1 should lead a
Snapback 4 while Codex / GPT-6 Astra leads Snapback 3 and Snapback 2
continues, "and see which one turns out best." The lead answered yes with
conditions; the author accepted them ("Sounds good") and asked for this
document. The decisions below are therefore the lead's proposal as accepted
by the author. Where the author has not separately ruled, the text says so.

1. **Three tracks, one comparison.** Snapback 2 continues as the incumbent
   and the control. Snapback 3 builds LLP 2000; Snapback 4 builds LLP
   2000.000. This is a bakeoff of *bets* — discover the substrates in the
   slice versus decide them first — not of models. The result will say more
   about the bets than about the leads, and §3 is written so that it can.
2. **The name is Snapback 4.** Siblings, not a sequence: "4" does not mean
   "after 3." A separate number gives the track its own corpus root,
   directory, package identity and data directory with no rename when the
   bakeoff resolves, and files never move in LLP. If Snapback 4 loses, it is
   a number that never shipped. "Snapback 3b" was considered and rejected:
   it reads as a variant of 3, and this is a different bet.
3. **Snapback 4 builds LLP 2000.000 as written.** Its §14 open questions are
   the lead's to decide inside the track, recorded in §7 as they are
   decided. The author rules on the bar, the comparison and promotion (§3).
4. **One owner on the core.** The lead writes the engine, the store and the
   client contract. Codex lanes build the fixture generator, the reference
   applications, the device witnesses and the periphery. Blind reviews from
   two model families are reserved for the two disclosure-sensitive
   mechanisms: partition inversion of read rules (LLP 2000.000 §7.1) and
   the meter's cut behaviour on non-uniform scans (LLP 2000.000 §6). This
   departs from the standing rule that Codex codes and Claude orchestrates,
   deliberately, for the core only.
5. **The clock is four weeks** from the first commit under `snapback4/` to
   the first slice running on the hub fixture. At the clock the author kills
   the track, merges its substrates into a survivor, or continues it. There
   is no third month without a ruling.
6. **Shared and not shared.** Shared with Snapback 3: the LLP corpus, the
   Clique harness and scorers, the fixtures, the briefs, and the simulator
   once either track has one. Not shared: code, except the Snapback 2 pieces
   both are expected to copy (LLP 2000.000 §5.1).
7. **Snapback 2 keeps shipping and freezes new substrate work for the
   duration.** Fix-forward, the loop bar, acquisition and papercuts continue;
   no new certificate axes, client custody mechanisms or ceiling changes.
   This is accepted here as a condition of the bakeoff. Making it binding on
   concurrent Snapback 2 sessions needs one line in `rules/NOT-DOING.md`'s
   Moves ledger, which this document does not write.
8. **Reading load.** This root, updated in place, and one append-only
   journal, `snapback4/JOURNAL.md`. Sub-LLPs only for a decision that must
   outlive the journal. No panels beyond decision 4's two reviews.

## 2. The bet on one page

**Keep verbatim from Snapback 2** (LLP 2000.000 §5.1): the rule language
with its uniformity and hoist analysis, `decided_true` and the
structural-fact refusals; one data language, Quarry, with `--` comments,
`require … else CODE`, `shape`, `merge`, `walk`, `chunk`; the value model
and its conformance corpus; typed read and write states with no silent state
and the refusal envelope `{code, family, site, message, rewrite, guide}`; the
successor classifier (widening additive, checked narrowing, exact projection
ownership); `deliver`'s engine-computed audience, tombstones and
`bootstrap`; `schedule`, `job`, `chunk` and rehearsal; personas; `init`,
`dev`, `check`, `doctor`, `run`, `test`, `why`, `trace`, `guide`; H0
`deploy`, `rollback`, `secret`; channels; assets; the word-prefix index.

**Flip 1 — certify authorization, meter resources** (LLP 2000.000 §6). The
certificate keeps every read site's shape, uniformity, completeness class
and prediction eligibility, and stops pricing bytes and steps. A
deterministic meter charges examined rows, index entries, rule probes,
steps, heap, output and rows written as the program runs, on the server and
in the client interpreter alike. A query cut by the meter returns `capped`
with no continuation past the cut, the same observable outcome as a static
ceiling cut today; a mutation aborts with `E_BOUND` naming actual work; a
job is chunked. `check --cost` prints the structural cost class; `test`
prints measured meters on the hub fixture.

**Flip 2 — the viewer's partition is the unit of sync** (LLP 2000.000 §7).
`sync to <audience> last N by <index>` on a table makes the engine maintain,
per principal, the rows that principal may read, as a change log with a
horizon; a table without the word is online-only, and `check` says which
rule term prevented inversion. The client holds a replica of its partitions,
one watermark each, a durable outbox with client-minted ids, and the
interpreter; queries run locally and are `complete` inside the horizon.
Revocation is a scope tombstone; `until-revoked` and `delivered-history`
survive as the client's leave policy. Two client tiers — online-only
without a store, local-first with one — share one language and one server.

**The vocabulary is fixed before the mechanism** (LLP 2000.000 §8):

```ts
[fresh Codex round 7](reviews/3000.037.000-srv1-codex-round7.md). An actual static
supervisor build in the hermetic Ubuntu userspace accepts a living parent and
closes its inherited output pipe on launcher death without /proc.
## 9. Wave-33 day-two reading and reruns (DX16)

The day2 card stays within 8,000 UTF-8 bytes and walks save-v1, change-specific
routes, preview, v2 deploy, rollback, forward, frozen callers and production
accounts. Init adaptation prints it first; a preview does not record reading
history. Routes ask for at most two further sections. The card says which
references to leave closed. Large reference cards flatten into independently
bounded sections at paragraph/fence boundaries; --full lists them rather than
bypassing the budget. Selection uses the existing project-local content hashes;
--repeat alone forces a selected reread. An oversized executable account example
is a packed reference file behind a pointer, not an exception to the budget.
The compiled source corpus retains complete examples. Social conversation/jump
references also stay behind their own packed source pointers. The day2 card
includes compiling backfill/privacy/alias forms and creation-versus-edit authority
spelling; preliminary real-model over-reading motivated these additions. Publication extractors
explicitly iterate bounded sections when they need the full reference corpus.

Before edits, init --adapt --day2 --apply captures v1/ source modules and its
These reviews do not establish performance, qualification or author acceptance.


## 18. Bounded package reference and wiring lookups (TOK6)

The 0.4.48 rerun showed that relocating manuals merely relocated full reads.
The npm client now ships README as its only Markdown file, including nested
paths. Long reference sources stay in the repository and are compiled into the
CLI. `guide package-reference <server|client|following|offline-uploads|changes>`
lists bounded sections; `--section` selects one, `--find` searches and `--repeat`
forces a reread. No flag dumps an entire manual. Existing guide pointers and
installed consumers use these commands. SDK builds no longer install duplicate
Markdown signature digests under dist/guide-api; the CLI retains those digests.
Schema-less upgrades resolve the newly installed CLI in a fresh process and collect
its versioned, bounded history pages (older packages retain file compatibility).
History pagination preserves version ownership of every bullet; installed React
and starter proofs collect the same bounded reference pages.
The packed-file census measures the bytes/tokens of every installed Markdown
file plus a recursive listing, with a 3,000-token ceiling beyond README.

Inside a project with a bound schema, `guide api client/react/auth/generated`
opens with this app's GEN1 usage rather than a pointer to another read. It
compiles current Quarry read-only, without generating files or running security
campaigns; stale generated files cannot supply obsolete operation facts. Client
selects plain calls, React selects hooks, generated selects both, and auth shows
password setup plus each operation's shapes and caller guards/declared refusals. Job ownership,
private/null fields, service credentials, browser admission, Following/held reads,
page cursors and write/refusal distinctions retain GEN1's meaning. The four app
lookups share hash-only history for identical schema facts and separate plain/React
call blocks, so a batch does not repeat the packet; new calls/signatures still
print, changed facts print again, and --repeat forces the full selection. Other
cards retain independent history. Fact hashes retain their operation identity;
only emitted blocks become seen, and clipped new blocks remain available. Complementary
result/lifecycle signatures follow the packet; `--generic` explicitly opens the
SDK-only digest. Without a bound schema, module lookups retain generic signatures
and identify a broken schema; generated requires a bound project. The same app
packet underlies default/section/index reads, with the existing 8,000-byte card
budget and paging for larger apps. This is presentation, never authorization.

+    use super::*;
+    const TEXT: &str = "# Session\n\nA complete fact.\n\n```js\nrun();\n```\n";
+    fn root(label: &str) -> std::path::PathBuf {
+        let root = std::env::temp_dir().join(format!("guide1-{label}-{}", std::process::id()));
+        let _ = std::fs::remove_dir_all(&root);
+        std::fs::create_dir_all(&root).unwrap();
+        root
+    }
+    #[test]
+    fn inactivity_refresh_expiry_future_and_legacy_history() {
+        let root = root("expiry");
+        let read = |now| render_at(&root, "session", TEXT, false, 8000, now);
+        assert_eq!(read(1), TEXT);
+        let repeat = read(SESSION_MILLIS);
+        assert_eq!(repeat, TEXT);
+        assert!(!repeat.contains("--repeat") && !repeat.contains("already shown"));
+        assert_eq!(read(2 * SESSION_MILLIS), TEXT, "expiry uses the refreshed last read");
+        assert_eq!(read(1), TEXT, "future timestamps are invalid");
+        std::fs::write(root.join(".snapback4/guide-seen.json"), "[]").unwrap();
+        assert_eq!(read(2), TEXT, "legacy hash arrays start fresh");
+        std::fs::write(root.join(".snapback4/guide-seen.json"), "broken").unwrap();
+        assert_eq!(read(3), TEXT);
+        std::fs::remove_dir_all(root).unwrap();
+    }
+    #[test]
+    fn freshly_copied_history_never_suppresses_a_new_projects_read() {
+        let original = root("source"); let copied = root("copy");
+        render_at(&original, "session", TEXT, false, 8000, 100);
+        std::fs::create_dir_all(copied.join(".snapback4")).unwrap();
+        std::fs::copy(original.join(".snapback4/guide-seen.json"), copied.join(".snapback4/guide-seen.json")).unwrap();
+        assert_eq!(render_at(&copied, "session", TEXT, false, 8000, 101), TEXT);
+        let history = std::fs::read_to_string(copied.join(".snapback4/guide-seen.json")).unwrap();
+        assert!(!history.contains("complete fact") && !history.contains(copied.to_str().unwrap()));
+        std::fs::remove_dir_all(original).unwrap(); std::fs::remove_dir_all(copied).unwrap();
+    }
+    #[test]
+    fn concurrent_writes_are_atomic_and_never_hide_unemitted_content() {
+        let root = root("concurrent");
+        for _ in 0..12 {
+            let barrier = std::sync::Barrier::new(4);
+            std::thread::scope(|scope| {
+                let handles: Vec<_> = (0..4).map(|i| {
+                    let root = &root; let barrier = &barrier;
+                    scope.spawn(move || {
+                        barrier.wait();
+                        let text = format!("# Card {i}\n\nFact {i}.\n");
+                        let shown = render_at(root, &format!("card{i}"), &text, false, 8000, 100);
+                        assert!(shown.contains(&format!("Fact {i}.")));
+                    })
+                }).collect();
+                for handle in handles { handle.join().unwrap(); }
+            });
+            let h: History = serde_json::from_slice(&std::fs::read(root.join(".snapback4/guide-seen.json")).unwrap()).unwrap();
+            assert_eq!(h.last_read_ms, 100);
+            assert!(h.hashes.iter().all(|hash| hash.len() == 64));
+            assert_eq!(std::fs::read_dir(root.join(".snapback4")).unwrap().count(), 1);
+        }
+        std::fs::remove_dir_all(root).unwrap();
+    }
+}

     4	// @ref LLP 3000.007#20.1 — the ~200 aliases moved behind `guide aliases`; the bare listing stays one screen.
     5	pub fn listing() -> String {
     6	    format!("cards: {}\nwith use server: {}\nBy task: snapback4 guide tasks (exact commands for sign-in, roles, uploads, deadlines and proofs).\nStart here: snapback4 guide start (one compiling schema; read feature sections on demand).\nExact signatures: guide api proof | client | auth | generated. Do not read the cheatsheet before start.\nCards over {FULL_LINES} lines or {FULL_BYTES} bytes print content (opening and first section) plus a truncation hint and section index. Narrow with guide <card> --section <heading or number> prints one (a topic word prints the section that mentions it most), --find <words> prints the lines holding every word (a|b or a OR b for either), --full prints the whole card up to {REFERENCE_BYTES} bytes; larger cards explain the limit and list sections.\nMessaging app: snapback4 new <dir> --template social; guide social carries the client recipes.\nBackground notifications: snapback/workers/<service>.mjs runs with dev and serve; guide effects shows ledger: true and provider idempotency.\nAliases: {} words open a card, or one section of it (pagination, roles, capacity, aggregates, …); snapback4 guide aliases lists them.", KEYWORDS.iter().filter(|word| !SERVER_KEYWORDS.contains(word)).copied().collect::<Vec<_>>().join(", "), SERVER_KEYWORDS.join(", "), ALIASES.len())
     7	}

     9	/// `guide aliases`: every alias, one per line, with the card (and section) it opens.
    10	pub fn aliases() -> String {
    11	    let mut out = format!("{} aliases; `snapback4 guide <alias>` prints what it names (`--full` lists bounded sections for large cards):\n", ALIASES.len());
    12	    for (word, target) in ALIASES {
    13	        match super::alias_section(word) {
    14	            Some((_, heading)) => out.push_str(&format!("{word} -> {target} --section {heading:?}\n")),
    15	            None => out.push_str(&format!("{word} -> {target}\n")),
    16	        }
    17	    }
    18	    out.trim_end().to_owned()
    19	}

    21	/// The section number (1-based, as the index numbers it) and heading whose
    22	/// words start with `heading`'s: how an alias's section is found.
    23	fn numbered_heading(card: &str, heading: &str) -> Option<(usize, String)> {
    24	    let wanted = words_of(heading);
    25	    sections(card).into_iter().enumerate().find(|(_, s)| words_of(&s.1).starts_with(&wanted)).map(|(n, s)| (n + 1, s.1))
    26	}
    27	
    28	/// `guide <alias>` for an alias that names a section of `card` (the card
    29	/// `name`, as printed): the title, a line saying where this is and how to
    30	/// read the rest, then the section. `None` for an alias of a whole card.
    31	pub fn alias_landing(word: &str, name: &str, card: &str) -> Option<String> {
    32	    let (target, heading) = super::alias_section(word)?;
    33	    if target != name { return None; }
    34	    let (n, heading) = numbered_heading(card, heading)?;
    35	    let (_, _, start, end) = sections(card).into_iter().nth(n - 1)?;
    36	    let lines: Vec<&str> = card.lines().collect();
    37	    let title = lines.first().filter(|line| line.starts_with("# ")).copied().unwrap_or_default();
    38	    Some(format!("{title}\n({} -> guide {name}, section {n}. {heading}; the complete card (when it fits): snapback4 guide {name} --full · its sections: --sections)\n\n{}",
    39	        word.trim(), lines[start..end].join("\n").trim_end()))
    40	}

   260	/// 124 lines at most, stays whole).
   261	pub const FULL_LINES: usize = 240;
   262	/// Dense cards with very long lines need the same bounded first read.
   263	pub const FULL_BYTES: usize = 8_000;
   264	/// Explicit reads fit evolution (~11 KB), day2 (~8 KB) and staging (~17 KB).
   265	/// Large references still page; default/first-read budgets remain unchanged.
   266	// @ref LLP 3000.037.000#8 — explicit complete cards and heading-sized sections.
   267	pub const REFERENCE_BYTES: usize = 18_000;
   268	
   269	/// Explicit --full always reprints a complete card or explains why it must page.
   270	pub fn full(name: &str, card: &str) -> String {
   271	    if card.len() <= REFERENCE_BYTES { return card.to_owned(); }
   272	    format!("guide {name}: too long to print whole ({} bytes; --full limit {REFERENCE_BYTES} bytes). Read one section with --section <number or words>.\n\n{}", card.len(), section_index(name, &super::reader_card(name, card)))
   273	}
   274	
   275	/// Bound explicit selections separately from first/default reads.
   276	pub fn bounded_reference(name: &str, text: &str) -> String {
   277	    bounded(name, text, REFERENCE_BYTES)
   278	}
   279	/// The most opening lines the overview prints before its section index.
   280	const OPENING_LINES: usize = 40;
   281	
   282	/// Lowercase words, punctuation and backticks dropped: `sync to` == "Sync to".
   283	fn words_of(text: &str) -> String {
   284	    text.chars().map(|c| if c.is_alphanumeric() { c.to_ascii_lowercase() } else { ' ' }).collect::<String>().split_whitespace().collect::<Vec<_>>().join(" ")
   285	}
   286	
   287	/// Where the opening (the text before the first `##`) ends.
   288	fn opening_end(card: &str, found: &[(usize, String, usize, usize)]) -> usize {
   289	    found.first().map(|s| s.2).unwrap_or_else(|| card.lines().count())
   290	}
   291	
   292	fn index_lines(card: &str, found: &[(usize, String, usize, usize)]) -> String {
   293	    let opening = opening_end(card, found);
   294	    let mut out = String::new();
   295	    if opening > 5 { out.push_str(&format!("    0. Opening ({opening} lines)\n")); }
   296	    for (n, (level, heading, start, end)) in found.iter().enumerate() {
   297	        out.push_str(&format!("{}{:>2}. {heading} ({} lines)\n", "   ".repeat(level - 1), n + 1, end - start));
   298	    }
   299	    out
   300	}

   307	pub fn overview(name: &str, card: &str) -> Option<String> { overview_with(name, card, true) }

    33	    for block in guide::reading_blocks(&recipe) {

//! Declaration digests are generated by the client build; no manually copied signatures.
// @ref LLP 3000.037.000#8-wave-32-builder-reading-and-waiting-tok1 — on-demand module shapes replace d.ts greps.
pub fn render(module: &str, full: bool) -> Result<String, String> {
    if matches!(module, "client" | "react" | "auth" | "generated") {
        let cwd = std::env::current_dir().map_err(|e| e.to_string())?;
        if let Some(project) = crate::project::Project::locate(&cwd) {
            let bound = project.compile().map_err(|e| e.to_string())?;
            if bound.diagnostics.is_empty() {
                let digest = if module == "generated" {
                    String::new()
                } else {
                    generic(module, false)?
                };
                return usage(&bound, module, &digest);
            }
            if module == "generated" {
                return Err(
                    "guide api generated: run snapback4 check to repair the schema first".into(),
                );
            }
            return Ok(format!(
                "App schema does not bind; run snapback4 check. Generic signatures follow.\n\n{}",
                generic(module, full)?
            ));
        }
    }
    if module == "generated" {
        return Err(
            "guide api generated: run in your project with a bound snapback/*.q schema".into(),
        );
    }
    generic(module, full)
}

pub fn generic(module: &str, full: bool) -> Result<String, String> {
    let rendered: Result<String, String> = match module {
        "app" if full => Ok(include_str!("../../snapback4-lang/src/guide/api_app_full.md").into()),
        "app" => Ok(include_str!("../../snapback4-lang/src/guide/api_app.md").into()),
        "witness" if full => {
            Ok(include_str!("../../snapback4-lang/src/guide/api_witness_full.md").into())
        }
        "witness" => Ok(include_str!("../../snapback4-lang/src/guide/api_witness.md").into()),
        "react" if full => {
            Ok(include_str!("../../snapback4-lang/src/guide/api_react_full.md").into())
        }
        "react" => Ok(include_str!("../../snapback4-lang/src/guide/api_react.md").into()),
        "proof" if full => {
            Ok(include_str!("../../snapback4-lang/src/guide/proof_api_reference.md").into())
        }
        "proof" => Ok(include_str!("../../snapback4-lang/src/guide/api_proof.md").into()),
        "client" if full => {
            Ok(include_str!("../../snapback4-lang/src/guide/api_client_full.md").into())
        }
        "client" => Ok(include_str!("../../snapback4-lang/src/guide/api_client.md").into()),
        "auth" if full => {
            Ok(include_str!("../../snapback4-lang/src/guide/api_auth_full.md").into())
        }
        "auth" => Ok(include_str!("../../snapback4-lang/src/guide/api_auth.md").into()),
        "generated" => Err("guide api generated has app usage only; use guide api client --generic for SDK signatures".into()),
        _ => {
            Err("usage: snapback4 guide api <app|react|proof|client|auth|witness|generated>".into())
        }
    };
    let raw = rendered?;
    // Full generated declaration sets are one declaration per line. Separate
    // their fences so a large module can page without splitting TypeScript.
    Ok(
        if full && module != "proof" && raw.len() > snapback4_lang::guide::FULL_BYTES {
            let (intro, code) = raw
                .split_once("```ts\n")
                .ok_or("signature reference has no TypeScript fence")?;
            let (code, tail) = code
                .split_once("\n```")
                .ok_or("signature reference has no closing fence")?;
            format!(
                "{intro}{}\n{tail}",
                code.lines()
                    .map(|line| format!("```ts\n{line}\n```\n\n"))
                    .collect::<String>()
            )
        } else {
            raw
        },
    )
}

        format!(

   235	
   236	/// A card's `##`/`###` headings outside code fences: (level, heading, first line, end line).
   237	pub fn sections(card: &str) -> Vec<(usize, String, usize, usize)> {
   238	    let lines: Vec<&str> = card.lines().collect();
   239	    let mut fenced = false;
   240	    let mut found: Vec<(usize, String, usize, usize)> = Vec::new();
   241	    for (index, line) in lines.iter().enumerate() {
   242	        if line.trim_start().starts_with("```") { fenced = !fenced; continue; }
   243	        if fenced { continue; }
   244	        let level = line.chars().take_while(|c| *c == '#').count();
   245	        if !(2..=3).contains(&level) || !line[level..].starts_with(' ') { continue; }
   246	        found.push((level, line[level..].trim().to_owned(), index, lines.len()));
   247	    }
   248	    // A section runs to the next heading at its level or above.
   249	    for i in 0..found.len() {
   250	        let level = found[i].0;
   251	        if let Some(next) = found[i + 1..].iter().find(|next| next.0 <= level) { found[i].3 = next.2; }
   252	    }
   253	    found
   254	}
    let listing = String::from_utf8_lossy(&listing.stdout);
    assert!(listing.contains("--section <heading or number>") && listing.contains("--full"), "{listing}");
}

// Wave 3 (LLP 3000.037): 33 lanes lost batched card output to truncation. A card
// longer than a tool window prints its opening and a numbered index by default.
#[test]
fn a_long_card_prints_bounded_full_index_and_complete_selected_sections() {
    let guide = |args: &[&str]| { let o = Command::new(BIN).arg("guide").args(args).arg("--repeat").output().unwrap(); (o.status.code(), String::from_utf8_lossy(&o.stdout).into_owned()) };
    for keyword in snapback4_lang::guide::KEYWORDS {
        let (code, full) = guide(&[keyword, "--full"]);
        assert_eq!(code, Some(0), "{keyword}");
        let card = snapback4_lang::guide::card(keyword).unwrap();
        assert!(full.len() <= snapback4_lang::guide::REFERENCE_BYTES + 1, "{keyword}: --full keeps the hard reading budget");
        if card.len() > snapback4_lang::guide::REFERENCE_BYTES {
            let (_, index) = guide(&[keyword, "--sections"]);
            assert!(full.contains("too long to print whole") && full.ends_with(&index), "{keyword}: oversized --full explains its bound before the complete section index");
            let pages=index.lines().filter_map(|line| {
                let (number,label)=line.trim().split_once(". ")?;
                let number=number.parse::<usize>().ok()?;
                let heading=label.rsplit_once(" (").map_or(label,|(heading,_)|heading);
                Some((number,heading))
            }).collect::<Vec<_>>();
            assert!(!pages.is_empty(),"{keyword}: full index publishes its selectable sections");
            for (number,heading) in pages {
                let number=number.to_string();let (status,selected)=guide(&[keyword,"--section",&number]);
                assert_eq!(status,Some(0),"{keyword}: section {number}");
                if number!="0" {assert!(selected.contains(heading),"{keyword}: section {number} retains its heading");}
                assert!(selected.len()<=snapback4_lang::guide::REFERENCE_BYTES+1,"{keyword}: section {number} retains the byte budget");
                assert_eq!(selected.lines().filter(|line|line.trim_start().starts_with("```")).count()%2,0,"{keyword}: selected recipe fences are complete");
            }
        } else {
            assert_eq!(full.trim_end().lines().count(), card.trim_end().lines().count(), "{keyword}: a short full read keeps the complete card");
        }
        let (_, default) = guide(&[keyword]);
        if snapback4_lang::guide::alias_section(keyword).is_some() {
            assert!(default.contains("--full"), "{keyword}: section aliases retain their direct landing");
        } else if card.lines().count() > snapback4_lang::guide::FULL_LINES || card.len() > snapback4_lang::guide::FULL_BYTES {
            let title=card.lines().next().unwrap();
            assert!(default.starts_with(&format!("{title}\n(Truncated:")), "{keyword}: piped output explains its bounded view before content");
            let canonical = snapback4_lang::guide::resolve(keyword).unwrap_or(keyword);
            assert!(default.len() <= snapback4_lang::guide::FULL_BYTES + 1, "{keyword}: default output retains its hard byte budget");
            assert_eq!(default.lines().filter(|line| line.trim_start().starts_with("```" )).count() % 2, 0, "{keyword}: complete recipe fences survive the bound");
            let section_flag = if snapback4_lang::guide::sections(card).is_empty() { "--full" } else { "--section" };
            assert!(default.contains(&format!("snapback4 guide {canonical} {section_flag}")), "{keyword}: index names the supported selector");
            let (_, index) = guide(&[keyword, "--sections"]);
            assert!(index.contains("--section"),"{keyword}: explicit index routes the published bounded sections");
        } else if *keyword == "evolution" {
            assert_eq!(default.trim_end(),card.trim_end(),"evolution: the short default card stays complete");
        } else { assert_eq!(default, full, "{keyword}: short card prints in full"); }
    }
    // Sections resolve by number, any case, prefix and words; a miss names what mentions the words.
    let (_, by_name) = guide(&["types", "--section", "Engine-assigned per-parent sequences"]);
    let (_, type_index)=guide(&["types","--sections"]);
    let sequence_number=type_index.lines().find(|line|line.contains("Engine-assigned per-parent sequences")).unwrap().trim().split('.').next().unwrap();
    for words in [sequence_number, "ENGINE-ASSIGNED", "per-parent", "engine sequences"] {
        assert_eq!(guide(&["types", "--section", words]), (Some(0), by_name.clone()), "{words}");
    }
    // Wave 4: a topic word no heading names prints the section holding most of
    // its mentions, or every mention when they spread; only an unmentioned word refuses.
    let (code, topic) = guide(&["types", "--section", "Integer"]);
   if(module!=='auth'){
    assert.match(text,/Args: .*Result:/);assert.match(text,/possibly hidden\/absent; null/);
    assert.match(text,/pending is not committed/);assert.match(text,/Upload separately per column/);
   }
   const index=run(cwd,['guide','api',module,'--sections']);
   const n=[...index.matchAll(/^\s*(\d+)\. (?:query|mutation) /gm)][0]?.[1];
   assert.ok(n,`${module}: app section index`);
   const section=run(cwd,['guide','api',module,'--section',n,'--repeat']);
   assert.match(section,/## (?:query|mutation) /);assert.match(section,/Session:/);
   if(name==='cpap')assert.match(text,/DIRECTOR_ONLY/);
   console.log(name,module,Buffer.byteLength(text),encoding.encode(text).length);
  }
  const generic=run(cwd,['guide','api','client','--generic','--repeat']);assert.match(generic,/exact signature digest/);assert.doesNotMatch(generic,/## mutation /);
 assert.deepEqual(markdown.map(f=>f.path),['README.md']);
 const listing=pack.files.map(f=>f.path).join('\n');
 const text=listing+'\n'+markdown.filter(f=>f.path!=='README.md').map(f=>readFileSync(join(pkg,f.path),'utf8')).join('\n');
 const tokens=encoding.encode(text).length;
 assert.ok(tokens<=3000,`${tokens}/3000 tokens`);
 console.log('all packed paths plus non-README Markdown:',tokens,'tokens');
});

test('every embedded manual page is reachable, bounded, and keeps executable fences whole',()=>{
 for(const topic of ['server','client','following','offline-uploads','changes']){
  const index=run(root,['guide','package-reference',topic,'--sections']);
  const numbers=[...index.matchAll(/^\s*(\d+)\. /gm)].map(m=>m[1]);assert.ok(numbers.length);
  for(const n of numbers){const page=run(root,['guide','package-reference',topic,'--section',n,'--repeat']);assert.equal((page.match(/^```/gm)??[]).length%2,0,`${topic} ${n}: fence`);assert.doesNotMatch(page,/Truncated: default budget/);}
 }
});

# GUIDE1 — guide reading traps

Branch sb4-b49-guide1 created from fetched origin/main fdc4ecf78c in the reused sb4-b32-dx15 worktree. The pre-existing LEAN1 report was replaced as requested; no LEAN1 or DOCS1 commits were imported.

Orientation: LLP 3000 §7 and rules/RULES.md govern scope and affected verification. LLP 3000.037.000 §8 governs hash-only reading deltas and bounded defaults; §9 governs day-two paging and §18 package-reference custody. LLP 3000.007 §20.1 governs discovery/heading selection. No new app-authoring content or semantic surface is planned. Hand-off: none; update the existing implemented LLP.

Mechanism: 30-minute inactivity expiry from last_read_ms inside JSON (never mtime), plus canonical-project-path SHA-256 binding so an immediately copied cache starts fresh. Legacy/malformed/future state starts fresh. Only hashes and freshness metadata persist. Canonical aliases share identity; two readers in the same app inside 30 minutes share the inactivity session. Concurrent updates use unique atomic-replacement temporaries; lost history only causes a reread.

Explicit --full and sections get an 18,000-byte limit, enough for evolution/day2/staging (~10.9/8.2/16.9 KB), while larger cards retain indexes with a clear explanation. First/default limits remain 8,000 bytes/240 lines. Package-reference sections retain TOK6's 8,000-byte contract. Tests asserting the previous misleading full-index behavior and 8KB explicit-section ceiling are updated; first-read budgets remain.

Checks, replay, reviews and pushed commit: pending.
